# Hezbollah

For other uses, see [Hezbollah (disambiguation)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah_\(disambiguation\)).

"Hizbullah" redirects here. For the Indonesian militant group formed during the Japanese occupation, see [Hizbullah (Indonesia)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hizbullah_\(Indonesia\)).

**Hezbollah**[^cite_note-45] is a [Lebanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_people) [Shia Islamist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia_Islamism) political party with an active [paramilitary](146730.md) wing whose military and security activities have been prohibited by the Lebanese government since March 2026, amid the [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md).[^cite_note-46][^cite_note-Council-on-Foreign-Relations-43][^cite_note-47][^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50][^cite_note-51] Hezbollah's paramilitary wing is the [Jihad Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jihad_Council),[^cite_note-LevittP15-52] and its political wing is the [Loyalty to the Resistance Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loyalty_to_the_Resistance_Bloc) party in the [Lebanese Parliament](1618242.md). [Its armed strength](6222202.md) was assessed to be equivalent to that of a medium-sized army in 2016.[^cite_note-haaretz.com-53]

Hezbollah was founded in 1982 by Lebanese Shiite clerics in close cooperation with Iran.[^cite_note-54] It emerged in the context of the Lebanese civil war, the collapse of central authority, Iran's strategic interests in Lebanon, and the [Israeli invasion of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-bbc-hi-me2-55][^cite_note-56] The movement's formation was shaped by the [Iranian Revolution](347268.md) of 1979 and [Ayatollah](63899.md) [Ruhollah Khomeini](26232.md)'s model of Islamic governance. Hezbollah subsequently established strong ties with [Iran](14653.md) as part of the "[Axis of Resistance](36658251.md)".[^cite_note-57] The group was initially supported by 1,500 [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](1285472.md) (IRGC) instructors, who helped unify various Lebanese Shia factions under Hezbollah's leadership.[^cite_note-nybooks2-58] Hezbollah's 1985 manifesto outlined [its key objectives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ideology_of_Hezbollah), which include expelling Western influence from the region, [destroying Israel](40229800.md), pledging allegiance to [Iran's supreme leader](36814344.md), and establishing an [Islamic government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_republic) influenced by [Iran's political ideology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khomeinism). However, the manifesto also emphasised Lebanese self-determination.[^cite_note-59] Throughout the 1980s and 1990s, Hezbollah fought against Israeli forces and the [South Lebanon Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon_Army) (SLA), eventually leading to Israel's withdrawal from southern Lebanon in 2000.[^cite_note-60] Hezbollah also played a prominent role in the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War) and later became involved in the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md), where it fought alongside the government of [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md) against the ultimately victorious [Syrian opposition forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition_\(2011%E2%80%932024\)).[^cite_note-61]

In 2009, Hezbollah issued a new manifesto to oppose political sectarianism, broaden its appeal to non-Islamic movements, and promote a national unity government. The updated manifesto retained the same basic approach to foreign policy, emphasising what it characterizes as 'hegemonic strategies' of the U.S. and portraying Israel as its forward base for dominating the region.[^cite_note-Berti-62][^cite_note-reuters20091130-63]

Since the 1990s, Hezbollah has grown into a significant political force in Lebanon. The group operates a vast social services network, including schools and hospitals, and runs a satellite TV station, [Al-Manar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Manar). Politically, Hezbollah's Loyalty to the Resistance Bloc holds 15 seats in the Lebanese Parliament, making it a powerful player in Lebanon's government.[^cite_note-deeb-hzb-a-primer2-64] However, the group's influence has led to growing domestic criticism. Following the [2020 Beirut port explosion](64752725.md), Hezbollah was accused of obstructing efforts to hold those responsible accountable, contributing to a decline in public trust. A 2024 [Arab Barometer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Barometer) survey found that 55% of Lebanese have "no trust at all" in Hezbollah, although it remains popular among the Shia population.[^cite_note-65]

Despite calls for disarmament under [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) resolutions, Hezbollah has expanded its military capabilities. In 2013, its armed wing is considered stronger than the [Lebanese Armed Forces](17779.md),[^cite_note-NYT052020132-66] making it one of the most powerful non-state actors in the world. Hezbollah leader [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) declared in 2021 that the group had 100,000 fighters.[^cite_note-67] Hezbollah has been involved in several high-profile attacks; it is believed to be responsible for the [bombing of the US embassy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_US_embassy_bombing_in_Beirut) and the [US and French barracks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Beirut_barracks_bombings) bombings in Beirut in 1983, the [assassination of Rafic Hariri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Rafic_Hariri) in 2005,[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69] as well as later attacks, including bombings and hijackings.[^cite_note-70][^cite_note-71] While Hezbollah has been regarded as a [resistance movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resistance_movement) by some scholars,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFarida20191-2-72][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDaher20198-73][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Aloosy202043,_74-74] the entire organisation, or its military wing alone, has been designated as a [terrorist group](63578.md)[^cite_note-Rudoren-Jodi-2013b2-75] by at least 28 countries as of April 2026, including most Western countries.[^cite_note-76]

Since October 2023, Hezbollah has been [at war with Israel](75005425.md). During this war, Nasrallah [was assassinated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hezbollah_headquarters_strike) after 32 years of leading the group, along with other key members of Hezbollah leadership. The conflict has led to the [2024 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_War), which saw Southern Lebanon invaded by Israel, followed by [a ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement). On 7 August 2025, in a government meeting specifically addressing the [disarmament of Hezbollah](80353105.md), the majority of the government voted to approve the decision. The Lebanese Army was tasked to create a plan ensuring that only the state has control over weapons in Lebanon. The decision is based on a US plan to disarm Hezbollah. On 5 September, the Lebanese cabinet approved the [Homeland Shield Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homeland_Shield_Plan?action=edit\&redlink=1), the LAF's roadmap for the disarmament of all militias in Lebanon and specifically Hezbollah.[^cite_note-aljazeera20250905-77][^cite_note-apnews20250905-78] On 2 March 2026, Lebanese Prime Minister [Nawaf Salam](21077090.md) stated that the organisation's military actions range outside of Lebanese law.[^cite_note-lorientlejour.com-79][^cite_note-lbcgroup.tv-80]

## History

Main article: [History of Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Hezbollah)

In 1982, Hezbollah was conceived by Lebanese clerics and funded by [Iran](14653.md) primarily to fight the [Israeli invasion of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-bbc-hi-me2-55] The 1982 and the [1978 Israeli invasions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1978_South_Lebanon_conflict) had created a humanitarian crisis in Lebanon; many villages in the south had been destroyed and large numbers of Shias had been displaced from their homes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Aloosy2020176-177-81] In addition, the Shia had long been underrepresented in Lebanese politics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFarida20192-82] Both these factors fostered resentment among the local Shia population, making them a fertile ground for recruitment.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Aloosy2020176-177-81] Hezbollah was set up by local Shia committees, under the leadership of [Ruhollah Khomeini](26232.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFarida20192-82] Its forces were trained and organised by a contingent of 1,500 [Iranian Revolutionary Guards](1285472.md) that arrived from Iran with permission from the [Syrian government](57134800.md), which [occupied Lebanon's eastern highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_occupation_of_Lebanon), permitted their transit to a base in the [Beqaa Valley](2162385.md).[^cite_note-nybooks-83]

Scholars differ as to when Hezbollah came to be a distinct entity. Various sources list the official formation of the group as early as 1982,[^cite_note-84][^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86] whereas Diaz and Newman maintain that Hezbollah remained an amalgamation of various violent Shia extremists until as late as 1985.[^cite_note-87] Another version states that it was formed by supporters of Sheikh [Ragheb Harb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ragheb_Harb), a leader of the southern Shia resistance killed by Israel in 1984.[^cite_note-88] Regardless of when the name came into official use, a number of Shia groups were slowly assimilated into the organisation, such as [Islamic Jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Jihad_Organization), [Organisation of the Oppressed on Earth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_of_the_Oppressed_on_Earth) and the [Revolutionary Justice Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary_Justice_Organization).[^cite_note-US-Department-of-State-1999-89] These designations are considered to be synonymous with Hezbollah by the US,[^cite_note-US-Department-of-State-1999-89] Israel,[^cite_note-90] and Canada.[^cite_note-Canada-Gazette-Part-II-2003-91]

According to [Robert Fisk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Fisk)[^cite_note-PitytheNation-92] and Israeli General Shimon Shapira,[^cite_note-93] the date of 8 June 1982, two days after the Israeli invasion of Lebanon, when 50 [Shiite](26961.md) militants ambushed an [Israel Defence Forces](39237.md) armoured convoy in [Khalde](17284436.md) south of Beirut, is considered by Hezbollah as the founding myth of their [military wing](6222202.md). It was in this battle, delaying the Israeli advance to Beirut for six days, that the future Hezbollah military chief [Mustafa Badreddine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Badreddine) made his name as a serious commander.[^cite_note-94] According to Shapira, the lightly armed Shia fighters managed to capture an Israeli armoured vehicle on that day and paraded it in the Revolutionary Guards' [forward operating base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forward_operating_base) in [Baalbek](58207.md), Eastern Lebanon. Fisk writes:

> Down at Khalde, a remarkable phenomenon had taken shape. The Shia militiamen were running on foot into the Israeli gunfire to launch grenades at the Israeli armour, actually moving to within 20 feet of the tanks to open fire at them. Some of the Shia fighters had torn off pieces of their shirts and wrapped them around their heads as bands of martyrdom as the [Iranian revolutionary guards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC) had begun doing a year before when they staged their first mass attacks against the [Iraqis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_Army) in the [Gulf War a thousand miles to the east](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran-Iraq_War). When they set fire to one Israeli armoured vehicle, the gunmen were emboldened to advance further. None of us, I think, realised the critical importance of the events of Khalde that night. The Lebanese Shia were learning the principles of martyrdom and putting them into practice. Never before had we seen these men wear headbands like this; we thought it was another militia affectation but it was not. It was the beginning of a legend which also contained a strong element of truth. The Shia were now the Lebanese resistance, nationalist no doubt but also inspired by their religion. The party of God – in Arabic, the Hezbollah – were on the beaches of Khalde that night.[^cite_note-PitytheNation-92]

### 1980s

Main articles: [Lebanese civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_civil_war) and [South Lebanon conflict (1985–2000)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon_conflict_\(1985%E2%80%932000\))

Hezbollah emerged in [South Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon) during a consolidation of [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia_Islam_in_Lebanon) militias as a rival to the older [Amal Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Movement). Hezbollah played a significant role in the Lebanese civil war, opposing [US forces in 1982–83](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multinational_Force_in_Lebanon) and opposing Amal and [Syria](7515849.md) during the 1985–88 [War of the Camps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_the_Camps). However, Hezbollah's early primary focus was ending Israel's occupation of southern Lebanon[^cite_note-bbc-hi-me2-55] following Israel's 1982 invasion and siege of Beirut.[^cite_note-95] Amal, the main Lebanese Shia political group, initiated [guerrilla warfare](12720.md). In 2006, former Israeli prime minister [Ehud Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ehud_Barak) stated, "When we entered Lebanon ... there was no Hezbollah. We were accepted with perfumed rice and flowers by the Shia in the south. It was our presence there that created Hezbollah."[^cite_note-AN33-96]

Hezbollah waged an [asymmetric war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asymmetric_war) using [suicide attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Istishhad) against the [Israel Defence Forces](39237.md) (IDF) and Israeli targets outside of Lebanon.[^cite_note-pape-97] Hezbollah is reputed to have been among the first Islamic resistance groups in the Middle East to use the tactics of suicide bombing, assassination, and capturing foreign soldiers,[^cite_note-nybooks-83] as well as murders[^cite_note-HCR190-98] and hijackings.[^cite_note-Timeline:_Lebanon-99] Hezbollah also employed more conventional military tactics and weaponry, notably [Katyusha rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katyusha_rocket_launcher) and other missiles.[^cite_note-HCR190-98][^cite_note-100] At the end of the Lebanese Civil War in 1990, despite the [Taif Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taif_Agreement) asking for the "disbanding of all Lebanese and non-Lebanese militias", [Syria](7515849.md), which controlled Lebanon at that time, allowed Hezbollah to maintain their arsenal and control Shia areas along the border with Israel.[^cite_note-In_the_Party_of_God-101]

### After 1990

In the 1990s, Hezbollah transformed from a revolutionary group into a political one, in a process which has been described as the "Lebanonisation" of Hezbollah. Unlike its uncompromising revolutionary stance in the 1980s, Hezbollah conveyed a lenient stance towards the Lebanese state.[^cite_note-magnus98-102]

In 1992, Hezbollah decided to participate in elections, and [Ali Khamenei](385653.md), [supreme leader of Iran](36814344.md), endorsed it. Former Hezbollah secretary general, [Subhi al-Tufayli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subhi_al-Tufayli), contested this decision, which led to a schism in Hezbollah. Hezbollah won all twelve seats which were on its electoral list. At the end of that year, Hezbollah began to engage in dialogue with Lebanese Christians. Hezbollah regards cultural, political, and religious freedoms in Lebanon as sanctified, although it does not extend these values to groups who have relations with Israel.[^cite_note-103]

In 1997, Hezbollah formed the multi-confessional [Lebanese Brigades to Fight the Israeli Occupation](52552320.md) in an attempt to revive national and secular resistance against Israel, thereby marking the "Lebanonisation" of resistance.[^cite_note-104]

### Islamic Jihad Organisation (IJO)

Whether the [Islamic Jihad Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Jihad_Organisation) (IJO) was a *[nom de guerre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nom_de_guerre)* used by Hezbollah or a separate organisation, is disputed. According to certain sources, IJO was identified as merely a "telephone organisation",[^cite_note-105][^cite_note-106] and whose name was "used by those involved to disguise their true identity".[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108][^cite_note-109][^cite_note-110][^cite_note-111] Hezbollah reportedly also used another name, "Islamic Resistance" (*al-Muqawama al-Islamiyya*), for attacks against Israel.[^cite_note-magnus97-112]<sup>: 67 </sup>

A 2003 US court decision found IJO was the name used by Hezbollah for its attacks in Lebanon, parts of the Middle East, and Europe.[^cite_note-113] The US,[^cite_note-USDbackground2801-114] Israel,[^cite_note-mfaGOV960411-115] and Canada[^cite_note-Canada-Gazette-Part-II-2003-91] consider the names "Islamic Jihad Organisation", "Organisation of the Oppressed on Earth" and the "Revolutionary Justice Organisation" to be synonymous with Hezbollah.[^cite_note-116]

### Axis of Resistance

The group receives substantial financial and military backing from [Iran](14653.md), by [smuggling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_smuggling_to_Lebanon) and other measures,[^cite_note-117][^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119] positioning itself as the leading member of the "[Axis of Resistance](36658251.md)", an alliance in opposition to [Israel](9282173.md) and [Western influence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westernization) in the Middle East.[^cite_note-Hubbard-2024-120] In mid-September 2024, Israel [detonated thousands of communication devices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_electronic_device_attacks) distributed to Hezbollah operatives, including pagers and walkie-talkies, which exploded simultaneously across Lebanon. The attacks exposed deep vulnerabilities in Hezbollah networks.[^cite_note-121] Following the outcome of the [2024 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Lebanon_War), the [2024 Syrian opposition offensives](78460726.md), the [downfall](78548066.md) of the [Assad regime](57134800.md) and the weakening of the Axis of Resistance, Hezbollah has withdrawn the majority of its military infrastructure from southern Lebanon, transferring control to the Lebanese army.[^cite_note-middleeasteye20250412-122][^cite_note-123][^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125] This move aligns with the November 2024 U.S.-brokered ceasefire agreement, which mandates Hezbollah's repositioning north of the Litani River and the deployment of approximately 5,000 Lebanese troops to the south. The withdrawal aims to reduce tensions along the Israel-Lebanon border and facilitate the return of displaced civilians. While Hezbollah has removed heavy weaponry, some fighters from southern villages remain with light arms. The situation remains delicate, with ongoing monitoring by international observers to ensure compliance with the ceasefire terms,[^cite_note-middleeasteye20250412-122][^cite_note-126] although Israel has violated the ceasefire terms in near-daily basis.[^cite_note-127]

### Disarmament

Main article: [Disarmament of Hezbollah](80353105.md)

As part of the ceasefire agreement that ended the 2024 Israeli invasion of Lebanon, and with mounting pressure from the US, the Lebanese Armed Forces have undertaken efforts to disarm Hezbollah, including in its traditional strongholds in southern Lebanon, with the aid of Israeli intelligence.[^cite_note-128][^cite_note-129] In May 2025, the Lebanese government had announced that the army had largely achieved its aim.[^cite_note-130][^cite_note-131] On 5 August 2025, the [Lebanese government](17775.md) declared that a plan would be scheduled for the disarmament of the militias after a six-hour cabinet meeting with president [Joseph Aoun](53467128.md).[^cite_note-132] According to the Lebanese government, Hezbollah has handed over control of several critical facilities.[^cite_note-133] Information minister [Paul Morcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Morcos) stated that a deadline of the end of 2025 was set for disarmament.[^cite_note-134]

Hezbollah leader [Naim Qassem](28105310.md) condemned the disarmament proposal, stating: "The resistance is fine, strong and ready to fight for Lebanon's sovereignty and independence... Hezbollah made heavy sacrifices to defend Lebanon against the Israeli aggression." He warned that Hezbollah was prepared to renew attacks on Israel and said all of Lebanon would unite to counter Israel.[^cite_note-135] On 22 August, Qassem threatened a [civil war](7085.md) if the disarmament proposal went forward, which prime minister [Nawaf Salam](21077090.md) condemned as "completely unacceptable".[^cite_note-136]

On 7 August, the cabinet of ministers approved the objective of the disarmament proposal, ensuring that arms in Lebanon are only possessed by the state.[^cite_note-137] Hezbollah and [Amal Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Movement)-affiliated ministers walked out of the cabinet meeting in protest,[^cite_note-138] and thousands of Hezbollah supporters rallied against the approval in the southern suburbs of Beirut.[^cite_note-139] On 5 September, the Lebanese army presented the disarmament plan to the government.[^cite_note-140] On 5 September 2025, General [Rodolphe Haykal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodolphe_Haykal) presented to the Lebanese cabinet the Homeland Shield Plan, its roadmap for the disarmament of all militias in Lebanon, specifically Hezbollah.[^cite_note-aljazeera20250905-77][^cite_note-apnews20250905-78]

### 2026

Main article: [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md)

On 2 March 2026, Lebanese Prime Minister [Nawaf Salam](21077090.md) condemned Hezbollah's rocket and drone strikes from southern Lebanon, calling them irresponsible acts outside the authority of the Lebanese state that endanger national security. While he emphasised that all military action must fall under the government's control, he has not formally declared Hezbollah outlawed, focusing instead on preventing unauthorised armed operations.[^cite_note-lorientlejour.com-79][^cite_note-lbcgroup.tv-80]

## Ideology

Main article: [Ideology of Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ideology_of_Hezbollah)



Ayatollah [Ruhollah Khomeini](26232.md)

The ideology of Hezbollah has been summarised as [Shia Islamist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shia_Islamist) [radicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_radicalism);[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142] Hezbollah follows the Islamic [Shia](26961.md) theology developed by Iranian leader [Ayatollah](63899.md) Ruhollah Khomeini.[^cite_note-nybooks-83] Hezbollah was largely formed with the aid of the Khomeini's followers in the early 1980s in order to spread [Islamic revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_revolution)[^cite_note-Wright-2006-143] and follows a distinct version of Islamic Shia ideology ([*Wilayat al-faqih*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hokumat-e_Islami_:_Velayat-e_faqih_\(book_by_Khomeini\)) or Guardianship of the Islamic Jurists) developed by Khomeini, who was the leader of the "Islamic Revolution" in [Iran](14653.md).[^cite_note-HG20Ak02-144][^cite_note-mfaGOV960411-115] Although Hezbollah originally aimed to transform Lebanon into an [Islamic republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_republic), this goal has been abandoned in favour of a more inclusive approach.[^cite_note-bbc-hi-me2-55]

Early on, Hezbollah was influenced by ideas from prominent Sunni Islamists. Hezbollah's own rhetoric was Islamist in general, rather than Shia in particular.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Aloosy202078-79-145] Hezbollah's position on the Sunni-Shia divide was that instead of dwelling on theological differences, Sunnis and Shias ought to cooperate with one another to oppose the Israeli occupation of Arab lands.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Aloosy202078-79-145] To maintain a sense of Muslim unity, Hezbollah avoided direct criticism of Saudi Arabia; even during the [2007 Lebanon's conflict with the Salafis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2007_Lebanon_conflict), Al-Manar TV's employees had instructions "not to talk badly about Saudi Arabia".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Aloosy202080-146] This changed, however, after the beginning of the [Saudi-led intervention in the Yemeni civil war](46230181.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Aloosy202080-146]

Hezbollah has been described as [socially conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_conservatism).[^cite_note-social_conservative-147] It is [against homosexuality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homophobia) and [LGBT rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBTQ_rights_by_country_or_territory).[^cite_note-france24.com-148] In 2023, Hezbollah leader Hassan Nasrallah called for gay people to be killed and said that the LGBT community was a "threat to society".[^cite_note-149][^cite_note-150] The organisation also encourages women to wear traditional [Islamic veils](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_veiling_practices_by_country), especially the full-body [chador](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chador).[^cite_note-151]

Some scholars consider Hezbollah to have become a left-wing political movement;[^cite_note-daher-152] including political scientists Anisseh van Engeland and Rachael Rudolph,[^cite_note-engeland-153] [Imad Salamey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imad_Salamey), [Amal Saad-Ghorayeb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Saad-Ghorayeb), and [As'ad Abu Khalil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/As'ad_AbuKhalil).[^cite_note-daher-152] Salamey described Hezbollah as "a revolutionary proletarian party with an Islamic manifesto".[^cite_note-154] Feminist [Judith Butler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Butler) controversially called Hezbollah part of the "global left" because they define themselves as anti-imperialist.[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156] Communist writer [Nahla Chahal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahla_Chahal) wrote that Hezhollah "is a movement of the [Theology of Liberation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_theology#Palestinian_liberation_theology)".[^cite_note-157]

### 1985 manifesto

On 16 February 1985, Sheik Ibrahim al-Amin issued Hezbollah's manifesto. The ideology presented in it was described as radical. Its first objective was to [fight against](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-imperialism) what Hezbollah described as [US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_imperialism) and [Israeli imperialism](59076253.md), including the [Israeli occupation of Southern Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_Southern_Lebanon_\(1982%E2%80%932000\)) and other territories. The second objective was to gather all Muslims into an "[ummah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ummah)", under which Lebanon would further the aims of the [1979 Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1979_Revolution) of Iran. It also declared it would protect all Lebanese communities, excluding those that collaborated with Israel, and support all national movements—both Muslim and non-Muslim—throughout the world. The manifesto by Hezbollah rejects the existence of the state of Israel, calling for its destruction.[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159][^cite_note-160]

Translated excerpts from Hezbollah's original 1985 manifesto read:

> We are the sons of the *umma* (Muslim community) ... ... We are an *ummah* linked to the Muslims of the whole world by the solid doctrinal and religious connection of Islam, whose message God wanted to be fulfilled by the Seal of the [Prophets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prophets_of_Islam), i.e., Prophet [Muhammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad). ... As for our culture, it is based on the Holy [Quran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quran), the [Sunna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunnah) and the legal rulings of the *[faqih](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faqih)* who is our source of imitation ...[^cite_note-The_Hizballah_Program-161]

### 2009 manifesto

On 30 November 2009, secretary-general [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) presented a new manifesto at Hezbollah's 7th political conference.[^cite_note-reuters20091130-63] Besides its introduction, this 32-page document has three chapters on U.S. hegemonic strategies and the impact of [globalisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization); Hezbollah's approach to Lebanon; and Palestinian resistance to Israel.[^cite_note-Berti-62] While self-described as a "rebirth" document, the manifesto conveys the same basic approach to foreign policy as in the past, according to scholar [Benedetta Berti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benedetta_Berti), though it is more "politically savvy" and appeals to non-Islamic movements. Still, the manifesto offers new support for Lebanese political institutions, through which it had been making inroads. Notably, it says that it opposes political "sectarianism", idealises a national unity government, and treats the Lebanese army less as an enemy and more as a subordinate military arm.[^cite_note-Berti-62]

### Attitudes, statements, and actions concerning Israel and Zionism

See also: [Foreign relations of Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Hezbollah)

From the inception of Hezbollah to the present,[^cite_note-The_Hizballah_Program-161][^cite_note-UN_document-162] [the elimination of the State of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calls_for_the_destruction_of_Israel) has been one of Hezbollah's primary goals. Some translations of Hezbollah's 1985 Arabic-language manifesto state that "our struggle will end only when this entity \[Israel] is obliterated".[^cite_note-The_Hizballah_Program-161] According to Hezbollah's Deputy-General, [Naim Qassem](28105310.md), the struggle against Israel is a core belief of Hezbollah and the central rationale of Hezbollah's existence.[^cite_note-163]

Hezbollah says that its continued hostilities against Israel are justified as reciprocal to Israeli operations against Lebanon and as retaliation for what they claim is Israel's occupation of Lebanese territory.[^cite_note-164][^cite_note-165][^cite_note-166] Israel withdrew from Lebanon in 2000, and their withdrawal was verified by the United Nations as being in accordance with resolution 425 of 19 March 1978; however, Lebanon considers the [Shebaa farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_farms)—a 26 km<sup>2</sup> (10 sq mi) piece of land captured by Israel from Syria in the 1967 war and considered by the UN to be Syrian territory occupied by Israel—to be Lebanese territory.[^cite_note-SC/6878-167][^cite_note-168] Finally, Hezbollah considers Israel to be an illegitimate state. For these reasons, it justifies its actions as acts of defensive jihad.[^cite_note-Thisreen1999-1-169]

> If they go from Shebaa, we won't stop fighting them. ... Our goal is to liberate the 1948 borders of Palestine, ... The Jews who survive this war of liberation can [go back to Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Go_back_to_Poland) or wherever they came from. However, that the Jews who lived in Palestine before 1948 will be 'allowed to live as a minority and they will be cared for by the Muslim majority.'

— Hezbollah's spokesperson Hassan Ezzedin, about an Israeli withdrawal from Shebaa Farms

[^cite_note-In_the_Party_of_God-101]

### Attitudes and actions concerning Jews and Judaism

Main article: [Ideology of Hezbollah § Attitudes, statements, and actions concerning Jews and Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ideology_of_Hezbollah#Attitudes,_statements,_and_actions_concerning_Jews_and_Judaism)

Hezbollah officials have said, on rare occasions, that it is only "anti-Zionist" and not antisemitic.[^cite_note-Gleis-10] However, according to scholars, "these words do not hold up upon closer examination". Among other actions, Hezbollah actively engages in [Holocaust denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial) and spreads antisemitic conspiracy theories.[^cite_note-Gleis-10] [Muhammad Hussein Fadlallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Hussein_Fadlallah), a prominent Lebanese Shia cleric and an influential ideological figure associated with Hezbollah, made a number of statements expressing hostility toward Jews as a group.[^cite_note-170]

Various antisemitic statements have been attributed to Hezbollah officials.[^cite_note-171] [Amal Saad-Ghorayeb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Saad-Ghorayeb), a Lebanese political analyst, argues that although Zionism has influenced Hezbollah's [anti-Judaism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Judaism), "it is not contingent upon it because Hezbollah's hatred of Jews is more religiously motivated than politically motivated".[^cite_note-Saad-Ghorayeb-172] [Robert S. Wistrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_S._Wistrich), a historian specialising in the study of antisemitism, described Hezbollah's ideology concerning Jews:

> The anti-Semitism of Hezbollah leaders and spokesmen combines the image of seemingly invincible Jewish power ... and cunning with the contempt normally reserved for weak and cowardly enemies. Like the [Hamas](13913.md) propaganda for holy war, that of Hezbollah has relied on the endless vilification of Jews as 'enemies of mankind,' 'conspiratorial, obstinate, and conceited' adversaries full of 'satanic plans' to enslave the Arabs. It fuses traditional Islamic anti-Judaism with Western conspiracy myths, Third Worldist anti-Zionism, and Iranian Shiite contempt for Jews as 'ritually impure' and corrupt infidels. [Sheikh Fadlallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Fadlallah) typically insists ... that Jews wish to undermine or obliterate Islam and Arab cultural identity in order to advance their economic and political domination.[^cite_note-173]

Conflicting reports say [Al-Manar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Manar), the Hezbollah-owned and operated television station, accused either Israel or Jews of deliberately spreading HIV and other diseases to Arabs throughout the Middle East.[^cite_note-Block-174][^cite_note-175][^cite_note-176] Al-Manar was criticised in the West for airing "anti-Semitic propaganda" in the form of a television drama depicting a [Jewish world domination conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_world_domination).[^cite_note-177][^cite_note-178][^cite_note-179] The group has been accused by US analysts of engaging in Holocaust denial.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182] In addition, during its 2006 war, it apologised only for killing Israel's Arabs (i.e., non-Jews).[^cite_note-Gleis-10]

In November 2009, according to the [Jewish Telegraphic Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Telegraphic_Agency), Hezbollah pressured a private English-language school in western Beirut "which asked not to be identified", to eliminate from its [curriculum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curriculum) excerpts from *[The Diary of Anne Frank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Diary_of_Anne_Frank)*, a book of the writings from the diary kept by the Jewish child [Anne Frank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anne_Frank) whilst she was in hiding with her family during the [Nazi occupation of the Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_occupation_of_the_Netherlands).[^cite_note-183] This was after Hezbollah's member of Lebanese parliament [Hussein Hajj Hassan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_Hajj_Hassan), interviewed on the organisation's Al-Manar television channel, criticised the school for "showing poor judgment in picking out its textbooks", and [asked](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhetorical_question) how long Lebanon would "remain an open arena for the Zionist invasion of education".[^cite_note-184]

In *[The New Yorker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_Yorker)'*&#x73; 22 July 2024 issue, [Dexter Filkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dexter_Filkins), in his report on the border fight between Israel and the organisation, quoted a commander of Hezbollah, who had been active in its operations outside Lebanon, stating that the war between the "Zionist state" and the "party of God" would be "very simply" resolved, "when \[the Jews] leave on the same boat they came on".[^cite_note-boat-185]

## Organisation



Organisational chart of Hezbollah, by Ahmad Nizar Hamzeh



[Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md), the third Secretary General of Hezbollah

At the beginning, many Hezbollah leaders maintained that the movement was "not an organisation, for its members carry no cards and bear no specific responsibilities",[^cite_note-186] and that the movement does not have "a clearly defined organisational structure".[^cite_note-magnus97-112]<sup>: 41 </sup> Today, as Hezbollah scholar [Magnus Ranstorp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnus_Ranstorp) reports, Hezbollah does actually have a formal governing structure and, in keeping with the principle of [Guardianship of the Islamic Jurists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guardianship_of_the_Islamic_Jurists) (*velayat-e faqih*), it "concentrate\[s] ... all authority and powers" on its religious leaders, whose decisions, then, "flow from the *[ulama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulema)* down the entire community".

> The supreme decision-making bodies of the Hezbollah were divided between the Majlis al-Shura (Consultative Assembly) which was headed by 12 senior clerical members with responsibility for tactical decisions and supervision of overall Hizballah activity throughout Lebanon, and the Majlis al-Shura al-Karar (the Deciding Assembly), headed by Sheikh Muhammad Hussein Fadlallah and composed of eleven other clerics with responsibility for all strategic matters. Within the Majlis al-Shura, there existed seven specialised committees dealing with ideological, financial, military and political, judicial, informational and social affairs. In turn, the Majlis al-Shura and these seven committees were replicated in each of Hizballah's three main operational areas (the [Beqaa](866204.md), [Beirut](37428.md), and the South).[^cite_note-magnus97-112]<sup>: 45 </sup>

Since the [Supreme Leader of Iran](36814344.md) is the ultimate clerical authority, Hezbollah's leaders have appealed to him "for guidance and directives in cases when Hezbollah's collective leadership \[was] too divided over issues and fail\[ed] to reach a consensus".[^cite_note-magnus97-112]<sup>: 45 </sup> After the death of Iran's first Supreme Leader, Khomeini, Hezbollah's governing bodies developed a more "independent role" and appealed to Iran less often.[^cite_note-magnus97-112]<sup>: 45 </sup> Since the [Second Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Lebanon_War), however, [Iran](14653.md) has restructured Hezbollah to limit the power of [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md), and invested a significant sum of money "rehabilitating" Hezbollah.[^cite_note-187]

Structurally, Hezbollah does not distinguish between its political and social activities within Lebanon and its military/*jihad* activities against Israel. In 2009, [Naim Qassem](28105310.md), Hezbollah's then-second in command, said that "Hezbollah has a single leadership ... All political, social and jihad work is tied to the decisions of this leadership ... The same leadership that directs the parliamentary and government work also leads jihad actions in the struggle against Israel."[^cite_note-188]

In 2010, Iran's parliamentary speaker [Ali Larijani](531848.md) said, "Iran takes pride in Lebanon's Islamic resistance movement for its steadfast Islamic stance. Hezbollah nurtures the original ideas of Islamic Jihad." He also instead charged the West with having accused Iran with support of terrorism and said, "The real terrorists are those who provide the Zionist regime with military equipment to bomb the people."[^cite_note-189]

### Funding

Main article: [Funding of Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funding_of_Hezbollah)

See also: [Hezbollah–Iran relations](75617077.md) and [Drug economy in Lebanon § Hezbollah's involvement in the drug industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drug_economy_in_Lebanon#Hezbollah's_involvement_in_the_drug_industry)



Nasrallah visiting Iranian [Supreme Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_leader_of_Iran) [Ali Khamenei](385653.md) in Tehran, August 2005

Funding of Hezbollah comes from the Iranian government, Lebanese business groups, private persons, businessmen, the Lebanese diaspora involved in African diamond exploration, other Islamic groups and countries, taxes paid by the Shia Lebanese, and crime.[^cite_note-Engeland33-190][^cite_note-191] Hezbollah says that the main source of its income comes from its own investment portfolios and donations by Muslims.

Western sources maintain that Hezbollah receives most of its financial, training, weapons, explosives, political, diplomatic, and organisational aid from [Iran](14653.md) and [Syria](7515849.md).[^cite_note-In_the_Party_of_God-101][^cite_note-USDbackground2801-114][^cite_note-192] Iran is said to have given \$400 million between 1983 and 1989 through donations. Ostensibly on account of economic problems, Iran temporarily limited funds to humanitarian actions carried on by Hezbollah.[^cite_note-Engeland33-190] During the late 1980s, when there was [three-digit inflation in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Lebanon#Macro-economic_trend) due to the collapse of the [Lira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_pound), the British periodical [Middle East International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_International) reported that Hezbollah was receiving \$3–5 million per month from Iran.[^cite_note-193] According to reports subsequently released, Hezbollah received \$400 million from Iran.[^cite_note-irinnews52494-194][^cite_note-WPBestGuerrilla-195][^cite_note-Haaretz_746631-196]

In 2009, when the United States [GAO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GAO) agency accused members of the [Venezuelan government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuelan_government) of "not cooperating fully in [the war on drug trafficking](1181646.md)" and claimed that "drug corruption had reached the ministerial level in Venezuela", [Dorit Shavit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorit_Shavit), then in charge of Latin America & Caribbean affairs at the [Israeli foreign ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_foreign_ministry), stated in *[El Tiempo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Tiempo_\(Colombia\))* that the presence of "cells of Hezbollah guerrillas" had increased in recent years in the [Guajira Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guajira_Peninsula) and on the [island of Margarita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Island_of_Margarita). The foreign ministry of Venezuela rejected these allegations as "absurd".[^cite_note-hezven-197]

In 2011, Iran earmarked US\$7 million to Hezbollah's activities in Latin America.[^cite_note-nyd-198] Hezbollah has relied also on funding from the [Shi'ite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi'a_Islam_in_Lebanon) Lebanese Diaspora in West Africa, the United States and, most importantly, the [Triple Frontier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple_Frontier), or tri-border area, along the junction of Paraguay, Argentina, and Brazil.[^cite_note-199] US law enforcement officials have identified an illegal multimillion-US dollar [cigarette-smuggling fund raising operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Smokescreen),[^cite_note-200] and a [drug smuggling](218449.md) operation.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203] Nasrallah has repeatedly denied any links between the South American drug trade and Hezbollah, calling such accusations "propaganda" and attempts "to damage the image of Hezbollah".[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205]

As of 2018, annual Iranian monetary support for Hezbollah was estimated at US\$700 million by US officials.[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-207]

Israel's efforts against Hezbollah have focused on its military assets as well as its finances, with an emphasis on the estimated US\$700 million directed from Iran each year through intermediaries that accounted for most of Hezbollah's annual operating budget. Some of the earliest Israeli military efforts during the [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md) in March 2026 targeted the organization's supply of gold and foreign currency, as well as branches of the [Al-Qard Al-Hasan Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qard_Al-Hasan_Association), a non-profit financial institution that has been described as being "Hezbollah's bank in Lebanon". Money changing offices and gas stations were also struck, all as part of an effort to squeeze the organization's sources of funding. To bypass constraints set limiting the access of Al-Qard Al-Hasan to the formal banking network, the [hawala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawala) system had been used to send US\$1 billion in Iranian funds in 2025 via a network of broker transfers that passed through [Iraq](7515928.md), [Turkey](11125639.md) and the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md), though sanctions have been placed on these brokers by the [United States Department of the Treasury](53667.md).[^cite_note-208]

## Social services

Main article: [Hezbollah social services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah_social_services)

Hezbollah organises and maintains an extensive social development program and runs hospitals, news services, educational facilities, and encouragement of [Nikah mut'ah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nikah_mut'ah).[^cite_note-irinnews52494-194][^cite_note-209] One of its established institutions, Jihad Al Binna's Reconstruction Campaign, is responsible for numerous economic and infrastructure development projects in Lebanon.[^cite_note-sachs-210] Hezbollah controls the Martyr's Institute (Al-Shahid Social Association), which pays stipends to "families of fighters who die" in battle.[^cite_note-WPBestGuerrilla-195] An IRIN news report of the UN Office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs noted:

> Hezbollah not only has armed and political wings—it also boasts an extensive social development program. Hezbollah currently operates at least four hospitals, twelve clinics, twelve schools and two agricultural centres that provide farmers with technical assistance and training. It also has an environmental department and an extensive social assistance program. Medical care is also cheaper than in most of the country's private hospitals and free for Hezbollah members.[^cite_note-irinnews52494-194]

According to CNN, "Hezbollah did everything that a government should do, from collecting the garbage to running hospitals and repairing schools."[^cite_note-cnn20060724-1-211] In July 2006, during the war with Israel, when there was no running water in Beirut, Hezbollah was arranging supplies around the city. Lebanese [Shiites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi'a_Islam_in_Lebanon) "see Hezbollah as a political movement and a social service provider as much as it is a militia".[^cite_note-cnn20060724-1-211] Hezbollah also rewards its guerrilla members who have been wounded in battle by taking them to Hezbollah-run amusement parks.[^cite_note-212]

Hezbollah is, therefore, deeply embedded in [Lebanese society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_society).[^cite_note-nybooks-83]

Hezbollah along with [Amal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amal_Movement) is one of two major political parties in Lebanon that represent [Shiite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi'a_Islam) Muslims.[^cite_note-213] Unlike Amal, whose support is predominantly in Lebanon's south, Hezbollah maintains broad-based support in all three areas of Lebanon with a majority Shia Muslim population: in the south, in Beirut and its surrounding area, and in the northern [Beqaa Valley](2162385.md) and [Hermel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermel) region.[^cite_note-AN6-214]

Hezbollah holds 14 of the 128 seats in the [Parliament of Lebanon](1618242.md) and is a member of the [Resistance and Development Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resistance_and_Development_Bloc). According to Daniel L. Byman, it is "the most powerful single political movement in Lebanon".[^cite_note-215] Hezbollah, along with the Amal Movement, represents most of the [Lebanese Shiite community](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Shia_Muslims). Unlike Amal, Hezbollah has not disarmed. Hezbollah participates in the Parliament of Lebanon.

### Political alliances

Hezbollah has been one of the main parties of the [March 8 Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_8_Alliance) since March 2005. Although Hezbollah had joined the new government in 2005, it remained staunchly opposed to the [March 14 Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_14_Alliance).[^cite_note-216] On 1 December 2006, these groups began [a series of political protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006%E2%80%932008_Lebanese_political_protests) and [sit-ins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sit-in) in opposition to the government of Prime Minister [Fouad Siniora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fouad_Siniora).[^cite_note-Ghattas-217]

In 2006, [Michel Aoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Aoun) and [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) met in Mar Mikhayel Church, [Chiyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiyah), and signed a memorandum of understanding between [Free Patriotic Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Patriotic_Movement) and Hezbollah organising their relation and discussing Hezbollah's disarmament with some conditions. The agreement also discussed the importance of having normal diplomatic relations with [Syria](7515849.md) and the request for information about the Lebanese political prisoners in Syria and the return of all political prisoners and diaspora in [Israel](9282173.md). After this event, Aoun and his party became part of the March 8 Alliance.[^cite_note-Harris2012-218]

On 7 May 2008, [Lebanon](17771.md)'s [17-month-long political crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006%E2%80%932008_Lebanese_political_protests) spiralled out of control. [The fighting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2008_unrest_in_Lebanon) was sparked by a government move to shut down Hezbollah's telecommunication network and remove Beirut Airport's security chief over alleged ties to Hezbollah. Hezbollah leader [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) said the government's decision to declare the group's military telecommunications network illegal was a "declaration of war" on the organisation, and demanded that the government revoke it.[^cite_note-219]

Hezbollah-led opposition fighters seized control of several West Beirut neighbourhoods from [Future Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Future_Movement) militiamen loyal to the backed government, in street battles that left 11 dead and 30 wounded. The opposition-seized areas were then handed over to the [Lebanese Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Army).[^cite_note-Haaretz1-220] The army also pledged to resolve the dispute and has reversed the decisions of the government by letting Hezbollah preserve its telecoms network and re-instating the airport's security chief.[^cite_note-221]

At the end, rival Lebanese leaders reached consensus over [Doha Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doha_Agreement_\(2008\)) on 21 May 2008, to end the 18-month political feud that exploded into fighting and nearly drove the country to a new civil war.[^cite_note-france24-222] On the basis of this agreement, Hezbollah and its opposition allies were effectively granted veto power in Lebanon's parliament. At the end of the conflicts, [National unity government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_government_of_July_2008) was formed by [Fouad Siniora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fouad_Siniora) on 11 July 2008, with Hezbollah controlling one ministerial and eleven of thirty cabinet places.[^cite_note-CFR-223]

In [2018 Lebanese general election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_Lebanese_general_election), Hezbollah general secretary [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) presented the names of the 13 Hezbollah candidates.[^cite_note-hzb1-224] On 22 March 2018, Nasrallah issued a statement outlining the main priorities for the parliamentary bloc of the party, Loyalty to the Resistance, in the next parliament.[^cite_note-manary-225] He stated that rooting out corruption would be the foremost priority of the [Loyalty to the Resistance Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loyalty_to_the_Resistance_Bloc).[^cite_note-manary-225] The electoral slogan of the party was 'We will construct and we will protect'.[^cite_note-olj19-226] Finally Hezbollah held 12 seats and its alliance won the election by gaining 70 out of 128 seats of [Parliament of Lebanon](1618242.md).[^cite_note-reuters.com-227][^cite_note-aljazeera.com-228]

In October 2024, leader of the Free Patriotic Movement, [Gebran Bassil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gebran_Bassil), announced that the party was no longer in alliance with Hezbollah.[^cite_note-229] In February 2025, Lebanese Prime Minister [Nawaf Salam](21077090.md) announced his government, which consists of 24 ministers; the Hezbollah controls two portfolios; the Public Health Ministry, headed by Rakan Nasredine, and the Labour Ministry, headed by Muhammad Haidar. The Hizbullah and Amal Bloc controls together six portfolios in the government.[^cite_note-230]

According to a poll conducted by [Information International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Information_International,_Inc.) and published in May 2026 by Lebanese [*Al-Jadeed*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jadeed), about 58% of respondents supported state monopoly on arms and the disarmament of non-state actors such as Hezbollah. While 88% of Shi’ites and 70% of Sunni respondents opposed Hezbollah’s disarmament, 89% of Orthodox Christians, 87% of Maronites, and 77% of Druze- agreed it should be disarmed.[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]

### Media operations

Hezbollah operates a satellite television station, [Al-Manar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Manar) TV ("the Lighthouse"), and a radio station, [al-Nour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Nour) ("the Light").[^cite_note-CNN-233] Al-Manar broadcasts from Beirut, Lebanon.[^cite_note-CNN-233] Hezbollah launched the station in 1991[^cite_note-natrev812-234] with the help of Iranian funds.[^cite_note-meforum583-235] Al-Manar, the self-proclaimed "Station of the Resistance", (*qanat al-muqawama*) is a key player in what Hezbollah calls its "[psychological warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychological_warfare) against the [Zionist enemy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism)"[^cite_note-meforum583-235][^cite_note-236] and an integral part of Hezbollah's plan to spread its message to the entire [Arab world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world).[^cite_note-meforum583-235]

Hezbollah's television station Al-Manar airs programming designed to inspire suicide attacks in [Gaza](12047.md), the [West Bank](33209.md), and [Iraq](7515928.md).[^cite_note-In_the_Party_of_God-101][^cite_note-natrev812-234][^cite_note-237] Al-Manar's transmission in France is prohibited due to its promotion of [Holocaust denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial), a criminal offence in France.[^cite_note-238] The United States lists Al-Manar television network as a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-239] Al-Manar was designated as a "[Specially Designated Global Terrorist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Specially_Designated_Global_Terrorist) entity", and banned by the United States in December 2004.[^cite_note-js4134-240] It has also been banned by France, Spain, and Germany.[^cite_note-france_ban-241][^cite_note-comm_EC-242]

Hezbollah is also associated with [Al-Ahed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Ahed_\(newspaper\)) ("the Covenant"), a weekly newspaper and online platform that publishes news reports, editorials, and ideological material aligned with the organisation's positions. It was established in 1984.[^cite_note-olfa-243] It is the only media outlet which is openly affiliated with the organisation.[^cite_note-olfa-243]

Additionally, Hezbollah operates cultural centres, such as the "Imam Khomeini Cultural Centres", and research institutions that organise lectures, seminars, and study circles focusing on Shiʿi theology, political ideology, and narratives of resistance. These institutions are used for both public outreach and internal cadre education.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245]

Materials aimed at instilling principles of nationalism and Islam in children are an aspect of Hezbollah's media operations.[^cite_note-246] The Hezbollah Central Internet Bureau released two video games – *[Special Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Force_\(2003_video_game\))* in 2003 and a sequel, *[Special Force 2: Tale of the Truthful Pledge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Force_2:_Tale_of_the_Truthful_Pledge)*, in 2007 – in which players are rewarded with points and weapons for killing Israeli soldiers.[^cite_note-247] In 2012, Al-Manar aired a television special praising an 8-year-old boy who raised money for Hezbollah and said: "When I grow up, I will be a communist resistance warrior with Hezbollah, fighting the United States and Israel, I will tear them to pieces and drive them out of Lebanon, the Golan and Palestine, which I love very dearly."[^cite_note-248]

## Secret services

Hezbollah's secret services have been called as "one of the best in the world",[^cite_note-Engeland33-190] and have even infiltrated the [Israeli army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_army).[^cite_note-Engeland33-190] [Lebanese intelligence agencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Lebanese_intelligence_agencies) and [Iranian intelligence agencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Iranian_intelligence_agencies) often collaborate with Hezbollah's secret services.[^cite_note-Engeland33-190]

In the summer of 1982, Hezbollah's Special Security Apparatus was created by Hussein al-Khalil, now a "top political adviser to Nasrallah";[^cite_note-249] while Hezbollah's counterintelligence was initially managed by Iran's [Quds Force](4028355.md),[^cite_note-Wege2-250]<sup>: 238 </sup> the organisation continued to grow during the 1990s. By 2008, scholar Carl Anthony Wege writes, "Hizballah had obtained complete dominance over Lebanon's official state counterintelligence apparatus, which now constituted a Hizballah asset for counterintelligence purposes."[^cite_note-Wege-251]<sup>: 775 </sup> This close connection with Lebanese intelligence helped bolster Hezbollah's financial counterintelligence unit.[^cite_note-Wege-251]<sup>: 772, 775 </sup>

According to Ahmad Hamzeh, Hezbollah's counterintelligence service is divided into *Amn al-Muddad*, responsible for "external" or "encounter" security; and *Amn al-Hizb*, which protects the organisation's integrity and its leaders. According to Wege, *Amn al-Muddad* "may have received specialised intelligence training in Iran and possibly North Korea".[^cite_note-Wege-251]<sup>: 773–774 </sup> The organisation also includes a military security component, as well as an External Security Organisation (*al-Amn al-Khariji* or [Unit 910](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unit_910?action=edit\&redlink=1)) that operates covertly outside Lebanon.[^cite_note-Wege2-250]<sup>: 238 </sup>

Successful Hezbollah counterintelligence operations include thwarting the [CIA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CIA)'s attempted kidnapping of foreign operations chief Hassan Ezzeddine in 1994, the 1997 manipulation of a double agent that led to the [Ansariya ambush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ansariya_ambush), and the 2000 kidnapping of alleged Mossad agent Elhanan Tannenbaum.[^cite_note-Wege-251]<sup>: 773 </sup> In 2006, Hezbollah collaborated with the Lebanese government to detect Adeeb al-Alam, a former colonel, as an Israeli spy.[^cite_note-Wege-251]<sup>: 774 </sup> Hezbollah recruited IDF Lieutenant Colonel Omar al-Heib, who was convicted in 2006 of conducting surveillance for Hezbollah.[^cite_note-Wege-251]<sup>: 776 </sup> In 2009, Hezbollah apprehended Marwan Faqih, a garage owner who installed tracking devices in Hezbollah-owned vehicles.[^cite_note-Wege-251]<sup>: 774 </sup>

Hezbollah's counterintelligence apparatus uses electronic surveillance and intercept technologies. By 2011, Hezbollah counterintelligence began to use software to analyse cellphone data and detect espionage. Suspicious callers were then subjected to conventional surveillance. In the mid-1990s, Hezbollah was able to "download unencrypted video feeds from Israeli drones",[^cite_note-Wege-251]<sup>: 777 </sup> and Israeli [SIGINT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SIGINT) efforts intensified after the 2000 withdrawal from Lebanon. With possible help from Iran and the [Russian](25391.md) [FSB](385990.md), Hezbollah augmented its electronic counterintelligence capabilities, and succeeded in 2008 in detecting Israeli bugs near [Mount Sannine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Sannine) and in the organisation's fibre optic network.[^cite_note-Wege-251]<sup>: 774, 777–778 </sup>

## Armed strength

Main article: [Hezbollah armed strength](6222202.md)



Hezbollah fighters in southern Lebanon, May 2023

Hezbollah does not reveal its armed strength. The Dubai-based Gulf Research Centre estimated in 2006 that Hezbollah's armed wing comprises 1,000 full-time Hezbollah members, along with a further 6,000–10,000 volunteers.[^cite_note-Hezbollah_force-252] According to the Iranian *[Fars News Agency](5000099.md)*, Hezbollah has up to 65,000 fighters.[^cite_note-253] In October 2023, [*Al Jazeera*](30204052.md) cited Hezbollah expert Nicholas Blanford as estimating that Hezbollah has at least 60,000 fighters, including full-time and reservists, and that it had increased its stockpile of missiles from 14,000 in 2006 to about 150,000.[^cite_note-HarperCollins-2013-44] It is often described as more militarily powerful than the Lebanese Army.[^cite_note-washpost2013-254][^cite_note-255][^cite_note-NYT05202013-256] Israeli commander [Guy Tzur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guy_Tzur) called Hezbollah "by far the greatest guerrilla group in the world".[^cite_note-257]

In 2010, Hezbollah was believed to have 45,000 rockets.[^cite_note-258] IDF Chief of Staff [Gadi Eisenkot](5249926.md) said that Hezbollah possesses "tens of thousands" of long- and short-range rockets, drones, advanced computer encryption capabilities, as well as advanced defence capabilities like the [SA-6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SA-6) anti-aircraft missile system.[^cite_note-259]

Hezbollah possesses the [Katyusha-122](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katyusha_rocket_launcher) rocket, which has a range of 29 km (18 mi) and carries a 15 kg (33 lb) warhead. Hezbollah possesses about 100 long-range missiles. They include the Iranian-made [Fajr-3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fajr-3_\(artillery_rocket\)) and [Fajr-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fajr-5), the latter with a range of 75 km (47 mi), enabling it to strike the Israeli port of [Haifa](55606.md), and the [Zelzal-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zelzal-1), with an estimated 150 km (93 mi) range, which can reach [Tel Aviv](31453.md). Fajr-3 missiles have a range of 40 km (25 mi) and a 45 kg (99 lb) warhead. Fajr-5 missiles, extend to 72 km (45 mi), also hold 45 kg (99 lb) warheads.[^cite_note-Hezbollah_force-252] It was reported that Hezbollah is in possession of [Scud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scud) missiles that were provided to them by Syria.[^cite_note-260] Syria denied the reports.[^cite_note-261]

According to various reports, Hezbollah is armed with [anti-tank guided missiles](37517.md), namely, the Russian-made [AT-3 Sagger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT-3_Sagger), [AT-4 Spigot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT-4_Spigot), [AT-5 Spandrel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AT-5_Spandrel), [AT-13 Saxhorn-2 'Metis-M'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metis-M), [АТ-14 Spriggan 'Kornet'](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9M133_Kornet), [Iranian](14653.md)-made [Ra'ad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAAD_\(anti-tank_guided_missile\)) (version of AT-3 Sagger), Towsan (version of AT-5 Spandrel), [Toophan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toophan) (version of [BGM-71 TOW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BGM-71_TOW)), and European-made [MILAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MILAN) missiles. These weapons have been used against IDF soldiers, causing many of the deaths during the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War).[^cite_note-262] US courts said that North Korea provided armaments to Hezbollah during the 2006 war.[^cite_note-263] A small number of Saeghe-2s, an Iranian-made version of the [M47 Dragon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M47_Dragon), were also used in the war.[^cite_note-264]

For air defence, Hezbollah has anti-aircraft weapons that include the [ZU-23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZU-23) artillery and the man-portable, shoulder-fired [SA-7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strela_2) and [SA-18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/9K38_Igla) [surface-to-air missile](182664.md) (SAM).[^cite_note-265] One of the most effective weapons deployed by Hezbollah has been the [C-802](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-802) [anti-ship missile](140725.md).[^cite_note-266]

In April 2010, U.S. Secretary of Defence [Robert Gates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Gates) claimed that Hezbollah has far more missiles and rockets than the majority of countries, and said that Syria and Iran are providing weapons to the organisation. Israel also claims that Syria is providing the organisation with these weapons. Syria has denied supplying these weapons and views these claims as an Israeli excuse for an attack. Leaked cables from US diplomats suggest that the United States has been trying unsuccessfully to prevent Syria from "supplying arms to Hezbollah in Lebanon", and that Hezbollah has "amassed a huge stockpile (of arms) since its 2006 war with Israel"; the arms were described as "increasingly sophisticated".[^cite_note-twsDecZ16ad-267] Gates added that Hezbollah is possibly armed with chemical or [biological weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biological_agent), as well as 105 km (65 mi) anti-ship missiles that could threaten U.S. ships.[^cite_note-268]

As of 2017, the Israeli government believe Hezbollah had an arsenal of nearly 150,000 rockets stationed on its border with Lebanon.[^cite_note-269] Some of these missiles are said to be capable of penetrating cities as far away as [Eilat](66162.md).[^cite_note-Jpost-270] The IDF has accused Hezbollah of storing these rockets beneath hospitals, schools, and civilian homes.[^cite_note-Jpost-270] Hezbollah has used drones against Israel, by penetrating air defence systems, in a report verified by Nasrallah, who added, "This is only part of our capabilities."[^cite_note-271]

Israeli military officials and analysts have drawn attention to the experience and weaponry Hezbollah would have gained from the involvement of thousands of its fighters in the Syrian Civil War. "This kind of experience cannot be bought", said [Gabi Siboni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabi_Siboni), director of the military and strategic affairs program at the Institute for National Security Studies at [Tel Aviv University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv_University). "It is an additional factor that we will have to deal with. There is no replacement for experience, and it is not to be scoffed at."[^cite_note-272]

In 2026 the Alma institute estimated that Hezbollah stores 25,000 rockets and is capable of launching several dozens a day. Additionally it estimated that Hezbollah has several hundreds of advanced missiles, a thousand [one-way attack drones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One-way_attack_drone), and its manpower includes 40,000 regular militants, out of which 5,000 operate under the [Radwan Force](69191358.md) and 3,000 are experienced with launching anti-tank missiles, and trained for invading Israel through land and sea.[^cite_note-273]

Hezbollah used fiber optic drones in the [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md) as part of the [Hezbollah–Israel conflict](75005425.md).[^cite_note-274]

## Military activities

Main article: [Hezbollah military activities](7635103.md)

Hezbollah has a military branch known as the [Jihad Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jihad_Council),[^cite_note-275] one component of which is *Al-Muqawama al-Islamiyya* ("The Islamic Resistance"), and is the possible sponsor of a number of lesser-known militant groups, some of which may be little more than fronts for Hezbollah itself, including the Organisation of the Oppressed, the Revolutionary Justice Organisation, the Organisation of Right Against Wrong, and Followers of the Prophet Muhammad.[^cite_note-USDbackground2801-114] Some scholars have regarded Hezbollah as a [resistance movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resistance_movement).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFarida20191-2-72][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDaher20198-73][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAl-Aloosy202043,_74-74]

[United Nations Security Council Resolution 1559](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1559) called for the disarmament of militia[^cite_note-276] with the [Taif agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taif_agreement) at the end of the [Lebanese civil war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_civil_war). Hezbollah denounced, and protested against, the resolution.[^cite_note-277] The 2006 military conflict with Israel has increased the controversy. Failure to disarm remains a violation of the resolution and agreement as well as subsequent [United Nations Security Council Resolution 1701](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1701).[^cite_note-ADL-278] Since then both Israel and Hezbollah have asserted that the organisation has gained in military strength.[^cite_note-met-279]

A Lebanese public opinion poll taken in August 2006 shows that most of the [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi'a_Islam_in_Lebanon) did not believe that Hezbollah should disarm after the [2006 Lebanon war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_war), while the majority of Sunni, Druze and Christians believed that they should.[^cite_note-Briefing-280] The Lebanese cabinet, under president [Michel Suleiman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Suleiman) and Prime Minister [Fouad Siniora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fouad_Siniora), guidelines state that Hezbollah enjoys the right to "liberate occupied lands".[^cite_note-281] In 2009, a Hezbollah commander, speaking on condition of anonymity, said, "\[W]e have far more rockets and missiles \[now] than we did in 2006."[^cite_note-282]

### Lebanese Resistance Brigades

Main article: [Lebanese Resistance Brigades](52552320.md)

| Lebanese Resistance Brigades *Saraya al-Moukawama al-Lubnaniyya* |  |
| --- | --- |
| سرايا المقاومة اللبنانية |  |
|  |  |
| Leaders | Mohammed Aknan ([Beirut](37428.md))  Mohammad Saleh ([Sidon](28366.md)) [**†**](368513.md) |
| Dates active | 1998–2000 2009–present |
| Active regions | [Southern Lebanon](4205968.md), mainly [Sidon](28366.md) |
