The Lebanese Resistance Brigades ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): سرايا المقاومة اللبنانية, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic): *Sarāyā l-Muqāwama al-Lubnāniyya*), also known as the Lebanese Brigades to Resist the Israeli Occupation, were formed by Hezbollah in 1997 as a multi-faith (Christian, Druze, Sunni and Shia) volunteer force to combat the Israeli occupation of Southern Lebanon. With the Israeli withdrawal from Lebanon in 2000, the organisation was disbanded.[^cite_note-283]

In 2009, the Resistance Brigades were reactivated, mainly comprising Sunni supporters from the southern city of [Sidon](28366.md). Its strength was reduced in late 2013 from 500 to 200–250 due to residents' complaints about some fighters of the group exacerbating tensions with the local community.[^cite_note-284]

### The beginning of its military activities: the South Lebanon conflict

Main article: [South Lebanon conflict (1982–2000)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon_conflict_\(1982%E2%80%932000\))

Hezbollah has been involved in several cases of armed conflict with Israel: during the [1982–2000 South Lebanon conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982%E2%80%932000_South_Lebanon_conflict), Hezbollah waged a guerrilla campaign against Israeli forces occupying Southern Lebanon. In 1982, the [Palestine Liberation Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organisation) (PLO) was based in Southern Lebanon and was firing Katyusha rockets into northern Israel from Lebanon. Israel invaded Lebanon to evict the PLO, and Hezbollah became an armed organisation to expel the Israelis.[^cite_note-In_the_Party_of_God-101] Hezbollah's strength was enhanced by the dispatching of one thousand to two thousand members of the [Iranian Revolutionary Guards](1285472.md) and the financial backing of Iran.[^cite_note-lebarmy6915-285][^cite_note-286][^cite_note-287]

Iranian clerics, most notably Fzlollah Mahallati supervised this activity.[^cite_note-288] It became the main politico-military force among the Shia community in Lebanon and the main arm of what became known later as the Islamic Resistance in Lebanon. With the collapse of the [SLA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Lebanon_Army), and the rapid advance of Hezbollah forces, Israel withdrew on 24 May 2000 six weeks before the announced 7 July date."[^cite_note-Timeline:_Lebanon-99]

Hezbollah held a victory parade, and its popularity in Lebanon rose.[^cite_note-BBC_News-289] Israel withdrew in accordance with 1978's [United Nations Security Council Resolution 425](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_425).[^cite_note-SC/6878-167] Hezbollah and many analysts considered this a victory for the movement, and since then its popularity has been boosted in Lebanon.[^cite_note-BBC_News-289]

### Disputed attacks

Between 1982 and 1986, there were many attacks blamed on Hezbollah, although Hezbollah denied responsibility. Given that Hezbollah didn't officially exist at the time of many of these attacks (it officially came into existence in 1985[^cite_note-pbs20091024-290]) leads some scholars to be sceptical of implicating Hezbollah in these attacks.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDaher201970–71-291][^cite_note-pbs20091024-290] The first of these attacks were the [April 1983 US Embassy bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_1983_U.S._Embassy_bombing)[^cite_note-Ini-292] and [1983 Beirut barracks bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Beirut_barracks_bombing),[^cite_note-cfr_hezbollah-293] both attributed to Hezbollah by some Western intelligence agencies. Hezbollah denied responsibility for both the embassy bombing[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295] and the barracks bombing.[^cite_note-pbs20091024-290]

In the [Lebanon hostage crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon_hostage_crisis), 105 people were kidnapped between 1982 and 1992.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDaher201971-296] A variety of organisations took responsibility for these kidnappings: Organisation of Islamic Jihād for the Liberation of Palestine, Organisation for Revolutionary Justice, the Fajr Organisation, the Khaybar Brigade, and the Organisation of the World's Oppressed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDaher201971-296] Hezbollah denied kidnapping these individuals, but was blamed anyway.[^cite_note-297] Recent research has shown that the kidnappers were of various political backgrounds, and were often motivated by [familial feuds](292136.md) or were looking for [monetary ransom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ransom).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDaher201971-296][^cite_note-FOOTNOTENorton201861-298]

Since 1990, terror acts and attempts of which Hezbollah has been blamed include the following bombings and attacks against civilians and diplomats:

- The [1992 Israeli Embassy attack in Buenos Aires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_Israeli_Embassy_attack_in_Buenos_Aires), killing 29, in Argentina.

  [^cite_note-cfr_hezbollah-293]

  &#x20;Hezbollah operatives boasted of involvement.

  [^cite_note-Levitt_2013_102-299]

- The [1994 AMIA bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_AMIA_bombing) of a Jewish cultural centre, killing 85, in Argentina.

  [^cite_note-cfr_hezbollah-293]

  &#x20;[Ansar Allah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ansar_Allah_\(Lebanon\)), a Palestinian group closely associated with Hezbollah, claimed responsibility.

  [^cite_note-Levitt_2013_102-299]

- The [1994 AC Flight 901 attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alas_Chiricanas_Flight_901), killing 21, in Panama.

  [^cite_note-300]

  &#x20;Ansar Allah claimed responsibility.

  [^cite_note-Levitt_2013_102-299]

- The 1996 [Khobar Towers bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khobar_Towers_bombing), killing 19 US servicemen.

  [^cite_note-301]

- In 2002, [Singapore](27318.md) accused Hezbollah of recruiting Singaporeans in a failed 1990s plot to attack US and Israeli ships in the Singapore Straits.

  [^cite_note-fastfacts-302]

- 15 January 2008, bombing of a US Embassy vehicle in Beirut.

  [^cite_note-Stratfor-303]

- In 2009, a [Hezbollah plot in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Hezbollah_plot_in_Egypt) was uncovered, where Egyptian authorities arrested 49 men for planning attacks against [Israeli](9282173.md) and Egyptian targets in the [Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula).

  [^cite_note-304]

- The [2012 Burgas bus bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Burgas_bus_bombing), killing 6, in Bulgaria. Hezbollah denied responsibility.

  [^cite_note-Hezbollahblamed-305]

- Training Shia insurgents against US troops during the [Iraq War](5043324.md).

  [^cite_note-306]

### During the Bosnian War

Hezbollah provided fighters to fight on the Bosnian Muslim side during the [Bosnian War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosnian_War), as part of the broader Iranian involvement. "The Bosnian Muslim government is a client of the Iranians", wrote [Robert Baer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Baer), a CIA agent stationed in Sarajevo during the war. "If it's a choice between the CIA and the Iranians, they'll take the Iranians any day." By the war's end, public opinion polls showed some 86% Bosnian Muslims had a positive opinion of Iran.[^cite_note-307] In conjunction, Hezbollah initially sent 150 fighters to fight against the [Bosnian Serb Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Army_of_Republika_Srpska), the Bosnian Muslims' main opponent in the war.[^cite_note-Fisk-308] All Shia foreign advisors and fighters withdrew from Bosnia at the end of conflict.

### Conflict with Israel

Main article: [Iran–Israel proxy conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Israel_proxy_conflict)



Hezbollah members and supporters parade following the end of the [Israeli occupation of Southern Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_Southern_Lebanon_\(1982%E2%80%932000\)), May 2000

On 25 July 1993, following Hezbollah's killing of seven Israeli soldiers in southern Lebanon, Israel launched [Operation Accountability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Accountability), known in Lebanon as the Seven Day War, during which the IDF carried out their heaviest artillery and air attacks on targets in southern Lebanon since 1982. The aim of the operation was to eradicate the threat posed by Hezbollah and to force the civilian population north to Beirut so as to put pressure on the Lebanese Government to restrain Hezbollah.[^cite_note-OA-309] The fighting ended when an unwritten understanding was agreed to by the warring parties. Apparently, the 1993 understanding provided that Hezbollah combatants would not fire rockets at northern Israel, while Israel would not attack civilians or civilian targets in Lebanon.[^cite_note-310]

In April 1996, after continued Hezbollah rocket attacks on Israeli civilians,[^cite_note-MidEastWeb-311] the Israeli armed forces launched [Operation Grapes of Wrath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Grapes_of_Wrath), which was intended to wipe out Hezbollah's base in southern Lebanon. Over 100 Lebanese refugees were [killed by the shelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_shelling_of_Qana) of a UN base at [Qana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qana), in what the Israeli military said was a mistake.[^cite_note-312]

Following several days of negotiations, the two sides signed the [Grapes of Wrath Understandings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli-Lebanese_Ceasefire_Understanding) on 26 April 1996. A cease-fire was agreed upon between Israel and Hezbollah, which would be effective on 27 April 1996.[^cite_note-cobbanBR30_2-313] Both sides agreed that civilians should not be targeted, which meant that Hezbollah would be allowed to continue its military activities against IDF forces inside Lebanon.[^cite_note-cobbanBR30_2-313]

#### 2000 Hezbollah cross-border raid

Main article: [2000 Hezbollah cross-border raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2000_Hezbollah_cross-border_raid)

On 7 October 2000, three Israeli soldiers—Adi Avitan, Staff Sgt. Benyamin Avraham, and Staff Sgt. Omar Sawaidwere—were abducted by Hezbollah while patrolling the [border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Line_\(Lebanon\)) between the [Israeli-occupied](2781576.md) [Golan Heights](7515865.md) and [Lebanon](17771.md).[^cite_note-314] The soldiers were killed either during the attack or in its immediate aftermath.[^cite_note-CNN_Prisoner_Swap-315] Israel Defence Minister [Shaul Mofaz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shaul_Mofaz) said that Hezbollah abducted the soldiers and then killed them.[^cite_note-316] The bodies of the slain soldiers were exchanged for Lebanese prisoners in 2004.[^cite_note-CNN_Prisoner_Swap-315]

#### 2006 Lebanon War

Main article: [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War)



Hezbollah posters in the aftermath of the 2006 Lebanon War

The 2006 Lebanon War was a 34-day military conflict in Lebanon and northern Israel. The principal parties were Hezbollah [paramilitary](146730.md) forces and the Israeli military. The conflict was precipitated by a cross-border raid during which Hezbollah kidnapped and killed Israeli soldiers. [The conflict began](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zar'it-Shtula_incident) on 12 July 2006 when Hezbollah militants fired [rockets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_artillery#Rockets) at Israeli border towns as a diversion for an [anti-tank missile](37517.md) attack on two armoured [Humvees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humvee) patrolling the Israeli side of the border fence, killing three, injuring two, and seizing two Israeli soldiers.[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318]

Israel responded with [airstrikes](323167.md) and [artillery](2508.md) fire on targets in Lebanon that damaged Lebanese infrastructure, including Beirut's Rafic Hariri International Airport, which Israel said that Hezbollah used to import weapons and supplies,[^cite_note-319] an air and naval [blockade](293054.md),[^cite_note-320] and a ground invasion of [southern Lebanon](4205968.md). Hezbollah then launched more rockets into northern Israel and engaged the IDF in [guerrilla warfare](12720.md) from hardened positions.[^cite_note-321]

The war continued until 14 August 2006. Hezbollah was responsible for thousands of [Katyusha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katyusha_rocket_launcher) rocket attacks against Israeli civilian towns and cities in northern Israel,[^cite_note-322] which Hezbollah said were in retaliation for Israel's killing of civilians and targeting Lebanese infrastructure.[^cite_note-323] The conflict is believed to have killed 1,191–1,300 Lebanese citizens including combatants[^cite_note-independent.co.uk-324][^cite_note-325][^cite_note-Lebanon_2006_pp._3-6-326][^cite_note-327][^cite_note-LUS-328] and 165 Israelis including soldiers.[^cite_note-329]

#### 2010 gas field claims

In 2010, Hezbollah claimed that the Dalit and [Tamar gas field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamar_gas_field), discovered by [Noble Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noble_Energy) roughly 80 km (50 mi) west of [Haifa](55606.md) in Israeli exclusive economic zone, belong to Lebanon, and warned Israel against extracting gas from them. Senior officials from Hezbollah warned that they would not hesitate to use weapons to defend Lebanon's natural resources. Figures in the [March 14 Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_14_Forces) stated in response that Hezbollah was presenting another excuse to hold on to its arms. Lebanese MP [Antoine Zahra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antoine_Zahra_\(legislator\)) said that the issue is another item "in the endless list of excuses" meant to justify the continued existence of Hezbollah's arsenal.[^cite_note-330]

#### 2011 attack in Istanbul

In July 2011, Italian newspaper *[Corriere della Sera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corriere_della_Sera)* reported, based on US and Turkish sources,[^cite_note-consul-331] that Hezbollah was behind a bombing in Istanbul in May 2011 that wounded eight Turkish civilians. The report said that the attack was an assassination attempt on the Israeli consul to Turkey, Moshe Kimchi. Turkish intelligence sources denied the report and said, "Israel is in the habit of creating disinformation campaigns using different papers."[^cite_note-consul-331]

#### 2012 planned attack in Cyprus

Main article: [2012 Cyprus terrorist plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Cyprus_terrorist_plot)

In July 2012, a Lebanese man was detained by Cyprus police on possible charges relating to terrorism laws for planning attacks against Israeli tourists. According to security officials, the man was planning attacks for Hezbollah in Cyprus and admitted this after questioning. The police were alerted about the man due to an urgent message from Israeli intelligence. The Lebanese man was in possession of photographs of Israeli targets and had information on Israeli airlines flying back and forth from Cyprus, and planned to blow up a plane or tour bus.[^cite_note-Haaretz5-332] Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) stated that Iran assisted the Lebanese man with planning the attacks.[^cite_note-Ynet7-333]

#### 2012 Burgas attack

Main article: [2012 Burgas bus bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Burgas_bus_bombing)

Following an investigation into the 2012 Burgas bus bombing terrorist attack against Israeli citizens in [Bulgaria](3415.md), the Bulgarian government officially accused the Lebanese-militant movement Hezbollah of committing the attack.[^cite_note-reuters1-334] Five Israeli citizens, the Bulgarian bus driver, and the bomber were killed. The bomb exploded as the Israeli tourists boarded a bus from the airport to their hotel.

[Tsvetan Tsvetanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsvetan_Tsvetanov), Bulgaria's interior minister, reported that the two suspects responsible were members of the militant wing of Hezbollah; he said the suspected terrorists entered Bulgaria on 28 June and remained until 18 July. Israel had already previously suspected Hezbollah for the attack. Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) called the report "further corroboration of what we have already known, that Hezbollah and its [Iranian](14653.md) patrons are orchestrating a worldwide campaign of terror that is spanning countries and continents".[^cite_note-335] Netanyahu said that the attack in Bulgaria was just one of many that Hezbollah and Iran have planned and carried out, including attacks in Thailand, Kenya, Turkey, India, Azerbaijan, Cyprus and Georgia.[^cite_note-reuters1-334]

[John Brennan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_O._Brennan), Director of the Central Intelligence Agency, has said that, "Bulgaria's investigation exposes Hezbollah for what it is—a terrorist group that is willing to recklessly attack innocent men, women and children, and that poses a real and growing threat not only to Europe, but to the rest of the world."[^cite_note-jpost1-336] The result of the Bulgarian investigation comes at a time when Israel has been petitioning the European Union to join the United States in designating Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-jpost1-336]

#### 2015 Shebaa farms incident

Main article: [January 2015 Shebaa farms incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2015_Shebaa_farms_incident)

In response to [an attack against a military convoy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2015_Mazraat_Amal_incident) comprising Hezbollah and Iranian officers on 18 January 2015 at Quneitra in south of Syria, Hezbollah launched an ambush on 28 January against an Israeli military convoy in the Israeli-occupied [Shebaa Farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_Farms) with [anti-tank missiles](37517.md) against two Israeli vehicles patrolling the border,[^cite_note-337] killing 2 and wounding 7 Israeli soldiers and officers, as confirmed by Israeli military.

#### 2023–present Israel–Hezbollah conflict

Main article: [Israel–Hezbollah conflict (2023–present)](75005425.md)

On 8 October 2023, Hezbollah launched guided rockets and artillery shells at [Israeli-occupied](2781576.md) positions in [Shebaa Farms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shebaa_Farms) during the [Gaza war](74998519.md). Israel retaliated with [drone strikes](38212231.md) and artillery fire on Hezbollah positions near the [Golan Heights](7515865.md)–Lebanon border. The attacks came after Hezbollah expressed support and praise for the [Hamas](13913.md) attacks on Israel.[^cite_note-338][^cite_note-339] The clashes were the largest escalation between the two countries since the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War).

In November 2024, a [ceasefire deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement) was signed between Israel and Hezbollah to end 13 months of conflict. According to the agreement, Hezbollah was given 60 days to end its armed presence in southern Lebanon and Israeli forces were obliged to withdraw from the area over the same period.[^cite_note-340] In December 2024, [the fall of Assad's Baathist regime](78548066.md) in Syria was another blow to its Lebanese ally, Hezbollah, which was already weakened because of Israeli military actions.[^cite_note-341]

On 31 March 2025, a significant development took place as the [LAF](17779.md) and [UNIFIL](787766.md) forces entered a major Hezbollah weapons and storage camp in East Zawtar, near the Litani River—one of Hezbollah's largest and most strategic facilities in southern Lebanon. The move followed renewed tensions and Israeli threats to resume military operations, amid accusations of recent rocket fire toward [Kiryat Shmona](323369.md). Joint forces reportedly searched Hezbollah vehicles and found an empty missile launcher. It remains unclear whether Hezbollah consented to the operation. This action was seen as part of efforts to enforce UN Resolution 1701, which calls for disarming illegal groups south of the Litani and reinforcing the army's presence in the region.[^cite_note-342]

On 1 March 2026, for the first time since the joint [Israeli-US strikes against Iran](82546969.md), Hezbollah launched missiles and drones targeting northern and central Israel, as retaliation for the killing of [Ali Khamenei](385653.md). This led Israel to a series of major airstrikes on Southern Beirut (Dahieh), Beqaa Valley and areas near Tyre, targeting Senior Hezbollah operatives.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344][^cite_note-345]

#### Rearming of Hezbollah

Since the [2024 Israel–Lebanon ceasefire agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement), Hezbollah is rearming itself. According to Israeli and Arab intelligence sources, the organisation is using the Beirut seaport and its old smuggling land routes form Syria, for bringing in long range missiles, antitank missiles, artillery and rockets. It is also reported that Hezbollah is manufacturing weapons. The efforts take place mainly in the suburbs of Beirut and the [Beqaa Valley](2162385.md).[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347][^cite_note-348] On 16 July 2026, Syria's [Interior Ministry](34679004.md) said it had intercepted a shipment of long-range missiles, anti-tank missiles, and drones allegedly bound for Hezbollah after entering from the [Syrian–Iraqi border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian%E2%80%93Iraqi_border).[^cite_note-349]

### Assassination of Rafic Hariri

Main article: [Assassination of Rafic Hariri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_of_Rafic_Hariri)

On 14 February 2005, former Lebanese Prime Minister [Rafic Hariri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafic_Hariri) was killed, along with 21 others, when his motorcade was struck by a roadside bomb in Beirut. He had been PM during 1992–1998 and 2000–2004. In 2009, the UN special tribunal investigating the murder of Hariri reportedly found evidence linking Hezbollah to the murder.[^cite_note-350]

In August 2010, in response to notification that the UN tribunal would indict some Hezbollah members, [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) said Israel was looking for a way to assassinate Hariri as early as 1993 in order to create political chaos that would force Syria to withdraw from Lebanon, and to perpetuate an anti-Syrian atmosphere \[in Lebanon] in the wake of the assassination. He went on to say that in 1996 Hezbollah apprehended an agent working for Israel by the name of Ahmed Nasrallah—no relation to Hassan Nasrallah—who allegedly contacted Hariri's security detail and told them that he had solid proof that Hezbollah was planning to take his life. Hariri then contacted Hezbollah and advised them of the situation.[^cite_note-351] Saad Hariri responded that the UN should investigate these claims.[^cite_note-352]

On 30 June 2011, the [Special Tribunal for Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Tribunal_for_Lebanon), established to investigate the death of Hariri, issued [arrest warrants](146746.md) against four senior members of Hezbollah, including [Mustafa Badreddine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Badreddine).[^cite_note-BBC-30/6-353] On 3 July, [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md) rejected the indictment and denounced the tribunal as a plot against the party, vowing that the named persons would not be arrested under any circumstances.[^cite_note-BBC-3/7-354]

On 18 August 2020, the Special Tribunal for Lebanon found [Salim Ayyash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salim_Ayyash), a senior operative in Hezbollah, guilty in absentia of five charges including the intentional murder of Hariri with premeditation by using explosive materials.[^cite_note-355]

### Involvement in the Syrian Civil War

Further information: [Syrian Civil War spillover in Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Civil_War_spillover_in_Lebanon) and [Hezbollah involvement in the Syrian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah_involvement_in_the_Syrian_Civil_War)

See also: [Syrian Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_Hezbollah)

Hezbollah was a long time ally of the [Syrian Ba'athist regime](57134800.md), led by the [Al-Assad family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Assad_family) until its [downfall in 2024](78548066.md). Hezbollah has helped the [Syrian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_\(Syria\)) during the [Syrian civil war](30741795.md) in its fight against the [Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition_to_Bashar_al-Assad), which Hezbollah has described as a Zionist plot to destroy its alliance with al-Assad against Israel.[^cite_note-barnard-356] [Geneive Abdo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneive_Abdo) opined that Hezbollah's support for al-Assad in the Syrian war has "transformed" it from a group with "support among the Sunni for defeating Israel in a battle in 2006" into a "strictly Shia paramilitary force".[^cite_note-abdo-357] Hezbollah also fought against the [Islamic State](9087364.md).[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-359]

In August 2012, the United States sanctioned Hezbollah for its alleged role in the war.[^cite_note-360] General Secretary Nasrallah denied Hezbollah had been fighting on behalf of the Syrian government, stating in a 12 October 2012, speech that "right from the start the Syrian opposition has been telling the media that Hezbullah sent 3,000 fighters to Syria, which we have denied".[^cite_note-361] However, according to the Lebanese *Daily Star* newspaper, Nasrallah said in the same speech that Hezbollah fighters helped the Syrian government "retain control of some 23 strategically located villages \[in Syria] inhabited by Shiites of Lebanese citizenship". Nasrallah said that Hezbollah fighters have died in Syria doing their "jihadist duties".[^cite_note-362]

In 2012, Hezbollah fighters crossed the border from Lebanon and took over eight villages in the [Al-Qusayr District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Battle_of_al-Qusayr) of Syria.[^cite_note-alarabiya17feb13-363] On 16–17 February 2013, Syrian opposition groups claimed that Hezbollah, backed by the Syrian military, attacked three neighbouring Sunni villages controlled by the [Free Syrian Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Syrian_Army) (FSA). An FSA spokesman said, "Hezbollah's invasion is the first of its kind in terms of organisation, planning and coordination with the Syrian regime's air force." Hezbollah said three Lebanese Shiites, "acting in self-defence", were killed in the clashes with the FSA.[^cite_note-alarabiya17feb13-363][^cite_note-364] Lebanese security sources said that the three were Hezbollah members.[^cite_note-baalbekfigures-365] In response, the FSA allegedly attacked two Hezbollah positions on 21 February; one in Syria and one in Lebanon. Five days later, it said it destroyed a convoy carrying Hezbollah fighters and Syrian officers to Lebanon, killing all the passengers.[^cite_note-366]

In January 2013, a weapons convoy carrying [SA-17](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SA-17) anti-aircraft missiles to Hezbollah was [destroyed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2013_Rif_Dimashq_airstrike) allegedly by the [Israeli Air Force](177619.md). A nearby research centre for chemical weapons was also damaged. A similar [attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_2013_Rif_Dimashq_airstrikes) on weapons destined for Hezbollah occurred in May of the same year.

The leaders of the [March 14 alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_14_alliance) and other prominent Lebanese figures called on Hezbollah to end its involvement in Syria and said it is putting Lebanon at risk.[^cite_note-march14slam-367] [Subhi al-Tufayli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subhi_al-Tufayli), Hezbollah's former leader, said, "Hezbollah should not be defending the criminal regime that kills its own people and that has never fired a shot in defence of the Palestinians." He said, "those Hezbollah fighters who are killing children and terrorising people and destroying houses in Syria will go to hell."[^cite_note-368]

The Consultative Gathering, a group of Shia and Sunni leaders in [Baalbek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baalbek_District)-[Hermel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hermel_District), also called on Hezbollah not to "interfere" in Syria. They said, "Opening a front against the Syrian people and dragging Lebanon to war with the Syrian people is very dangerous and will have a negative impact on the relations between the two."[^cite_note-baalbekfigures-365] [Walid Jumblatt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walid_Jumblatt), leader of the [Progressive Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_Socialist_Party), also called on Hezbollah to end its involvement,[^cite_note-march14slam-367] and claimed that, "Hezbollah is fighting inside Syria with orders from Iran."[^cite_note-369]

Egyptian President [Mohamed Morsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Morsi) condemned Hezbollah by saying, "We stand against Hezbollah in its aggression against the Syrian people. There is no space or place for Hezbollah in Syria."[^cite_note-370] Support for Hezbollah among the Syrian public has weakened since the involvement of Hezbollah and Iran in propping up the [Assad regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assad_regime) during the [civil war](30741795.md).[^cite_note-371]

On 12 May 2013, Hezbollah with the Syrian army attempted to [retake part of Al-Qusayr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Battle_of_al-Qusayr).[^cite_note-Hezbollah_in_Q_2013-372] In Lebanon, there has been "a recent increase in the funerals of Hezbollah fighters" and "Syrian rebels have shelled Hezbollah-controlled areas".[^cite_note-Hezbollah_in_Q_2013-372]

On 25 May 2013, Nasrallah announced that Hezbollah is fighting in the Syrian Civil War against [Islamic extremists](15012.md) and "pledged that his group will not allow Syrian militants to control areas that border Lebanon".[^cite_note-Hezbollah_2013-373] He confirmed that Hezbollah was fighting in the strategic Syrian town of Al-Qusayr on the same side as Assad's forces.[^cite_note-Hezbollah_2013-373] In the televised address, he said, "If Syria falls in the hands of the US, Israel and the [takfiris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takfiris), the people of our region will go into a dark period."[^cite_note-Hezbollah_2013-373]

### Involvement in Iranian-led intervention in Iraq

Beginning in July 2014, Hezbollah sent an undisclosed number of technical advisers and intelligence analysts to Baghdad in support of the [Iranian intervention in Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%93present\)). Shortly thereafter, Hezbollah commander Ibrahim al-Hajj was reported killed in action near [Mosul](200475.md).[^cite_note-vox1-374]

### Latin America operations

Main article: [Hezbollah in Latin America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah_in_Latin_America)

Hezbollah operations in South America began in the late 20th century, centred around the Arab population which had moved there following the 1948 Arab-Israeli War and the 1985 Lebanese Civil War.[^cite_note-375] One particular form of alleged activity is [money laundering](19390.md).[^cite_note-376] The *[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times)* said that the group was more active in the 1990s, especially during the 1992 Israeli embassy bombing in Argentina, though its relevance grew more unclear as time progressed.[^cite_note-Linthicum-2020-377] [*Vox*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vox_\(website\)) writes that following the adoption of the [Patriot Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriot_Act) in 2001, the [Drug Enforcement Administration](146720.md) (DEA) would promote the term of [narcoterrorism](221350.md) and arrest individuals with no prior history of being involved in terrorism, suggesting scepticism towards the reports of large-scale collusion between alleged terrorist groups and cartels.[^cite_note-378]

In 2002, Hezbollah was reported to be openly operating in [Ciudad del Este](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ciudad_del_Este), Paraguay.[^cite_note-379] Beginning in 2008, the [DEA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DEA) began with [Project Cassandra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Cassandra) to work against reported Hezbollah activities in regards to Latin American drug trafficking.[^cite_note-380] The investigation by the DEA reported that Hezbollah made about a billion US dollars a year and trafficked thousands of tons of cocaine into the United States.[^cite_note-381] Another destination for cocaine trafficking done by Hezbollah are nations within the [Gulf Cooperation Council](220950.md) (GCC).[^cite_note-382] In 2013, Hezbollah was accused of infiltrating South America and having ties with Latin American drug cartels.[^cite_note-383]

One area of operations is in the region of the [Triple Frontier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple_Frontier), where Hezbollah has been alleged to be involved in the trafficking of cocaine; officials with the Lebanese embassy in Paraguay have worked to counter US allegations and extradition attempts.[^cite_note-384] In 2016, it was alleged that money gained from drug sales was used to purchase weapons in Syria.[^cite_note-385] In 2018, *[Infobae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infobae)* reported that Hezbollah was operating in [Colombia](5222.md) under the name Organisation of External Security.[^cite_note-386] That same year, Argentine police arrested individuals alleged to be connected to Hezbollah's criminal activities within the nation.[^cite_note-387]

The *[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times)* noted in 2020 that at the time, Hezbollah served as a "bogeyman of sorts" and that "\[p]undits and politicians in the U.S., particularly those on the [far right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far_right), have long issued periodic warnings that Hezbollah and other Islamic groups pose a serious threat in Latin America".[^cite_note-Linthicum-2020-377] Various allegations have been made that [Cuba](5042481.md),[^cite_note-388] [Nicaragua](21362.md)[^cite_note-Rogers-2012-389] and [Venezuela](32374.md)[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393] aid Hezbollah in its operations in the region.[^cite_note-394] Israeli reports about the presence of Hezbollah in Latin America raised questions amongst Latin American analysts based in the United States[^cite_note-Rogers-2012-389] while experts say that reports of presence in Latin America are exaggerated.[^cite_note-Linthicum-2020-377]

*[Southern Pulse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Pulse)* director and analyst [Samuel Logan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Logan) said "Geopolitical proximity to Tehran doesn't directly translate into leniency of Hezbollah activity inside your country" in an interview with the [Pulitzer Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulitzer_Center).[^cite_note-Rogers-2012-389] William Neuman in his 2022 book *[Things Are Never So Bad That They Can't Get Worse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Things_Are_Never_So_Bad_That_They_Can't_Get_Worse)* said that claims of Hezbollah's presence in Latin America was "in reality, minimal", writing that the Venezuelan opposition raised such allegations to persuade the United States into believing that the nation faced a threat from Venezuela in an effort to promote foreign intervention.[^cite_note-395]

### United States operations

Ali Kourani, the first Hezbollah operative to be convicted and sentenced in the United States, was under investigation since 2013 and worked to provide targeting and terrorist recruiting information to Hezbollah's [Islamic Jihad Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Jihad_Organisation) (IJO).[^cite_note-396] The organisation had recruited a former resident of [Minnesota](19590.md) and a military linguist, Mariam Tala Thompson, who disclosed "identities of at least eight clandestine human assets; at least 10 US targets; and multiple tactics, techniques and procedures" before she was discovered and successfully prosecuted in a US court.[^cite_note-397]

### Other

In 2010, [Ahbash](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahbash) and Hezbollah members were involved in a street battle which was perceived to be over parking issues, both groups later met to form a joint compensation fund for the victims of the conflict.[^cite_note-Yalib-398]

According to *[Reuters](18998750.md)*, in 2024, commanders from Hezbollah and Iran's IRGC were reported to be involved in Yemen, overseeing and directing [Houthi attacks on Red Sea shipping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_involvement_in_the_Israel%E2%80%93Hamas_war).[^cite_note-hez-399]

## Funding

Main article: [Funding of Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funding_of_Hezbollah)

Since it was established, Hezbollah has been heavily dependent on Iran's IRGC for funds and equipment that often arrive through Syria. According to US officials, it received an annual sum of up to US\$700 million.[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402][^cite_note-403] Since the 1990s it became part of the Lebanese government and financial systems, while expanding its activities overseas through various businesses, smuggling, and money laundering, mainly in [South America](26769.md) and [West Africa](67393.md).[^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406] According to Dr. Eitan Azani and Ms. Lorena Atiyas-Lvovsky at the [Reichman University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reichman_University) Institute of Counter Terrorism, the Lebanese Shiite diaspora in Africa and Latin America's [Triple Frontier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Triple_Frontier) send contributions or forced donations in cash and via other informal transfer systems.[^cite_note-407] Following the 2006 conflict with Israel, Iran increased its financial support of Hezbollah.[^cite_note-408] In 2016, the DEA's “[Project Cassandra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Project_Cassandra)” said that part of Hezbollah's security wing, called the “Business Affairs Component,” was helping to move drug money around, allegedly using this money to buy weapons for the war in Syria.[^cite_note-409] Western sanctions targeted Hezbollah financial channels and caused the organisation to shift to clandestine operations that included money laundering, and in recent years more into cryptocurrency.[^cite_note-410][^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412][^cite_note-413]

The latest reports in Canada, show that the country has long been a hub of Hezbollah's financial networks.[^cite_note-ml-414][^cite_note-cv-415] As of 2025, the organisation continues its activity in Canada, using car trade making a profit on cars shipped through the [Port of Montreal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Montreal).[^cite_note-ml-414][^cite_note-cv-415][^cite_note-416] In 2024 Canada launched Operation Vector in which it prevented the smuggling of 598 cars with the estimated value of \$34.5 million.[^cite_note-cv-415] Other financial networks include fake charities, money laundering, cryptocurrencies, money services businesses (MSBs) and informal value transfer systems (IVTS) like [hawalas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawala).[^cite_note-ml-414][^cite_note-cv-415]

Israeli asset-seizure orders and blockchain-analytics reports indicate that, in the year preceding the [attacks of 7 October 2023](74998317.md), Hezbollah, alongside [Hamas](13913.md) and [PIJ](13093253.md), raised funds via [cryptocurrencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cryptocurrencies).[^cite_note-hammil-417] During this period, Hezbollah is reported to have received roughly US\$3.7 million.[^cite_note-418] The groups may also have routed funds between each other's crypto wallets; PIJ and Hezbollah alone are thought to have exchanged up to US\$12 million in cryptocurrency since 2021.[^cite_note-hammil-417]

After the [2023–2025 war with Israel](75005425.md), Nicholas Blanford of the [Atlantic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Council) think tank, stated that Hezbollah is faced with a multifaceted crisis like none it had seen in the past, with its efforts to reconstitute itself being hampered by access to long-term finances.[^cite_note-broke-419] The strengthened national government, again in charge of reconstruction, stated that the much needed financial aid from foreign donors is conditioned on establishing a [state monopoly on arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monopoly_on_violence), which is still in part resisted by Hezbollah.[^cite_note-broke-419] This follows a statement from May 2025 by the [US State Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_State_Department) conditioning Washington's support of sustainable reconstruction on Hezbollah "laying down their arms."[^cite_note-broke-419]

Starting in February 2025 and following Israeli threats, Lebanon's government barred commercial flights between Beirut and Tehran, making it harder to smuggle funds from Iran. Israel's military claimed that Hezbollah used to bring in cash from Iran using civilian aircraft, and threatened to take action to stop flights. The IDF declared on 25 June 2025 that it had killed an Iranian official in charge of money transfers from Iran to armed groups in the region, after also assassinating a south Lebanese middleman whose currency exchange firm allegedly helped funnel some of this money to Hezbollah.[^cite_note-420]



Arrival of fuel from Iran to Lebanon in September 2021

During the September 2021 fuel shortage, Hezbollah received a convoy of 80 tankers carrying oil/diesel fuel from Iran.[^cite_note-421][^cite_note-422]

On 20 August 2026, the US imposed further sanctions on Hezbollah, including redesignating the group for what the [U.S. Treasury Department](53667.md) described as its “service to the Iranian regime under the command of Iran’s [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps-Qods Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Revolutionary_Guard_Corps'_Qods_Force) (IRGC-QF)”.[^cite_note-423] Ten individuals whom the U.S. accused of operating a network that smuggled cash to Hezbollah, also through couriers travelling between [Iran](14653.md), [Turkey](11125639.md), the [United Arab Emirates](69328.md) and [Lebanon](17771.md) were also targeted. According to the U.S. Treasury, the network had moved hundreds of millions of dollars to Hezbollah and helped it get foreign currency outside the banking system.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425]

## Attacks on Hezbollah leaders

Hezbollah has also been the target of bomb attacks and kidnappings. These include:

- In the [1985 Beirut car bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985_Beirut_car_bombing), Shia cleric [Mohammad Hussein Fadlallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Hussein_Fadlallah), alleged by some to be the "spiritual mentor" of Hezbollah, was targeted, but the assassination attempt failed.

- On 28 July 1989, Israeli commandos kidnapped Sheikh [Abdel Karim Obeid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdel_Karim_Obeid), the leader of Hezbollah.

  [^cite_note-426]

  &#x20;This action led to the adoption of UN Security Council resolution 638, which condemned all hostage takings by all sides.

- On 16 February 1992, Israeli helicopters attacked a motorcade in southern Lebanon, killing the Hezbollah leader [Abbas al-Musawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abbas_al-Musawi), his wife, son, and four others.

  [^cite_note-Timeline:_Lebanon-99]

- On 31 March 1995, Rida Yasin, also known as Abu Ali, was killed by a single rocket fired from an [Israeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Airforce) helicopter while in a car near [Derdghaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derdghaya) in the Israeli [security zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_occupation_of_Southern_Lebanon_\(1982%E2%80%932000\)) 10 km east of [Tyre](30796.md). Yasin was a senior military commander in southern Lebanon. His companion in the car was also killed. An Israeli civilian was killed and fifteen wounded in the retaliatory rocket fire.

  [^cite_note-427]

  [^cite_note-428]

- On 12 February 2008, [Imad Mughniyeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imad_Mughniyeh) was killed by a car bomb in [Damascus](8914.md), Syria.

  [^cite_note-429]

- On 3 December 2013, senior military commander [Hassan al-Laqis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_al-Laqis) was shot outside his home, 3 kilometres (2 mi) southwest of Beirut. He died a few hours later on 4 December.

  [^cite_note-430]

- On 18 January 2015, a group of Hezbollah fighters was targeted in [Quneitra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quneitra), with the Al-Nusra Front claiming responsibility. In this attack, for which Israel was also accused, Jihad Mughniyeh, son of Imad Mughniyeh, five other members of Hezbollah and an Iranian general of Quds Force, [Mohammad Ali Allahdadi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Ali_Allahdadi), were killed.

  [^cite_note-jpost-431]

  [^cite_note-Aljazeera_a-432]

  [^cite_note-aljazeera2-433]

- On 10 May 2016, an explosion near Damascus International Airport killed top military commander [Mustafa Badreddine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mustafa_Badreddine). Lebanese media sources attributed the attack to an Israeli airstrike. Hezbollah attributed the attack to [Syrian opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syrian_opposition_to_Bashar_al-Assad).

  [^cite_note-434]

  [^cite_note-435]

  [^cite_note-436]

- On 30 July 2024, an Israeli air strike in Beirut killed Hezbollah senior commander, a founding member of Hezbollah's armed wing, [Fuad Shukr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fuad_Shukr).

  [^cite_note-437]

- On 20 September 2024, [Ibrahim Aqil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahim_Aqil), a senior commander in the group's elite [Redwan Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redwan_Force), was killed in Israeli strike on Beirut.

  [^cite_note-438]

- On 27 September 2024, an airstrike by the [Israeli Air Force](177619.md) in [Dahieh](6082788.md) killed secretary general [Hassan Nasrallah](313436.md), the Hezbollah's supreme leader.

  [^cite_note-ap20240928-439]

  &#x20;[Ali Karaki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Karaki), Hezbollah's commander of the southern front since 1982, was killed in the same Israeli airstrike alongside Nasrallah.

  [^cite_note-440]

- On 1 October 2024, an airstrike by the [Israeli Air Force](177619.md) in [Dahieh](6082788.md) killed [Muhammad Jafar Qasir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Jafar_Qasir?action=edit\&redlink=1), a high-ranking member, widely recognised for his role in managing the group's financial and logistical networks.

  [^cite_note-441]

  [^cite_note-442]

- On 4 October 2024, an Israeli air strike on the southern suburbs of Beirut killed Head of Hezbollah's Executive Council, [Hashem Safieddine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashem_Safieddine), likely successor of Nasrallah as Hezbollah's next leader.

  [^cite_note-443]

- On 17 November 2024, Hezbollah's media relations chief, [Mohammad Afif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Afif), was killed in an Israeli airstrike on the [Syrian Ba'ath Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Socialist_Ba'ath_Party_%E2%80%93_Syria_Region) headquarters in Beirut.

  [^cite_note-444]

- On 3 December 2024, An Israeli airstrike on a car near Damascus killed Salman Jumaa, a senior Hezbollah figure responsible for liaising with the Syrian army.

  [^cite_note-445]

- On 1 April 2025, Hassan Bdeir, known as ‘Hajj Rabih,’ a key figure in the Hezbollah's structure related to the Palestinian cause, and his son, Ali Bdeir, both were killed during the Israeli strike on the southern suburbs of Beirut.

  [^cite_note-446]

## Targeting policy

After the [attacks on 11 September 2001](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September_11,_2001_attacks) in the US, Hezbollah condemned [al-Qaeda](1921.md) for targeting civilians in the [World Trade Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Center_\(1973%E2%80%932001\)),[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448] but remained silent on the attack on [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pentagon).[^cite_note-nybooks-83][^cite_note-wp_inside_the_mind-449] Hezbollah also denounced the [massacres in Algeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Algerian_massacres_of_the_1990s) by [Armed Islamic Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Islamic_Group), [Al-Gama'a al-Islamiyya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Gama'a_al-Islamiyya) attacks on tourists in [Egypt](8087628.md),[^cite_note-450] the murder of [Nick Berg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nick_Berg),[^cite_note-451] and [ISIL](9087364.md) [attacks in Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/November_2015_Paris_attacks).[^cite_note-452]

Although Hezbollah has denounced certain attacks on civilians, some people accuse the organisation of the bombing of an Argentine synagogue in 1994. Argentine prosecutor [Alberto Nisman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alberto_Nisman), Marcelo Martinez Burgos, and their "staff of some 45 people"[^cite_note-453] said that Hezbollah and their contacts in Iran were responsible for the [1994 bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMIA_Bombing) of a Jewish cultural centre in Argentina, in which "\[e]ighty-five people were killed and more than 200 others injured".[^cite_note-Argentine-454]

In August 2012, the United States State Department's counter-terrorism coordinator [Daniel Benjamin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Benjamin) said that Hezbollah "is not constrained by concerns about collateral damage or political fallout that could result from conducting operations there \[in Europe]".[^cite_note-Hezbollah_Europe_hit3-455][^cite_note-Hezbollah_Europe_hit-456][^cite_note-Hezbollah_Europe_hit1-457]

## Foreign relations

Main article: [Foreign relations of Hezbollah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Hezbollah)

Hezbollah has close relations with Iran.[^cite_note-458] It also had ties with the leadership in [Ba'athist Syria](57134800.md), specifically President [Hafez al-Assad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hafez_al-Assad) supported it, until his death in 2000.[^cite_note-459] It was also a close ally of his son [Bashar al-Assad](364813.md), and its leader pledged support to the embattled Syrian leader.[^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461] Although Hezbollah and [Hamas](13913.md) are not organisationally linked, Hezbollah provides military training as well as financial and moral support to the [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni) [Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_people) group.[^cite_note-462] Furthermore, Hezbollah was a strong supporter of the [Second Intifada](71717.md).[^cite_note-nybooks-83]

US and Israeli counter-terrorism officials claim that Hezbollah has (or had) links to [Al Qaeda](1921.md), although Hezbollah's leaders deny these allegations.[^cite_note-Gunaratna-463][^cite_note-464] Also, some al-Qaeda leaders, like [Abu Musab al-Zarqawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Musab_al-Zarqawi)[^cite_note-465] and [Wahhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wahhabism) clerics, consider Hezbollah to be heretics.[^cite_note-466] But United States intelligence officials speculate that there has been contact between Hezbollah and low-level al-Qaeda figures who fled [Afghanistan](737.md) for Lebanon.[^cite_note-467] However, [Michel Samaha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Samaha), Lebanon's former minister of information, has said that Hezbollah has been an important ally of the government in the war against terrorist groups, and described the "US attempt to link Hezbollah to al-Qaeda" to be "astonishing".[^cite_note-nybooks-83]

In April 2025, a [multinational investigation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hezbollah_drone_smuggling_investigation_\(2024%E2%80%932025\)?action=edit\&redlink=1) involving Spain, France, Germany, and the United Kingdom uncovered a Hezbollah logistics network operating in Europe. Authorities arrested multiple individuals connected to the procurement of drone components intended for explosive-laden UAVs. The parts matched those used by Hezbollah in attacks on Israel, highlighting the group's international supply chain and ongoing drone development efforts.[^cite_note-468][^cite_note-469]

### Public opinion

As of 2024, Hezbollah's support within Lebanon is limited, especially after being blamed for the [2020 Beirut port explosion](64752725.md) and the obstruction of accountability efforts. According to a 2024 [Arab Barometer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Barometer) survey, 55% of Lebanese have "no trust at all" in the group. Support remains concentrated primarily among the [Shiite population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Shia_Muslims).[^cite_note-470]

According to [Michel Samaha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Samaha), Lebanon's Minister of Information, Hezbollah is seen as "a legitimate resistance organisation that has defended its land against the Israeli occupying force, and consistently stood up to the Israeli army".[^cite_note-nybooks-83] Samaha was sentenced for smuggling explosives to carry out terrorist attacks in Lebanon with the help of the Syrian regime.[^cite_note-meo20feb-471][^cite_note-muir-472]

According to a survey released by the "Beirut Centre for Research and Information", of 800 citizens polled between 24 and 26 July 2006, during the [2006 Lebanon War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Lebanon_War), "showed 87% support for Hizbullah's \[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*] retaliatory attacks on northern Israel".[^cite_note-473] a rise of 29% from a similar poll conducted in February. More striking, however, was the level of support for Hezbollah's resistance from Sunni communities. "Eighty percent of [Christians](4796145.md) polled supported Hizbullah \[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*] along with 80% of [Druze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Druze) and 89% of [Sunnis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanese_Sunni_Muslims)."[^cite_note-474]

In a poll of Lebanese adults taken in 2004, 6% of respondents gave unqualified support to the statement "Hezbollah should be disarmed". 41% reported unqualified disagreement. A poll of Gaza Strip and West Bank residents indicated that 79.6% had "a very good view" of Hezbollah, and most of the remainder had a "good view". Polls of Jordanian adults in December 2005 and June 2006 showed that 63.9% and 63.3%, respectively, considered Hezbollah to be a legitimate resistance organisation. In the December 2005 poll, only 6% of Jordanian adults considered Hezbollah to be terrorist.[^cite_note-an-475]

A July 2006 *[USA Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Today)*/[Gallup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup,_Inc.) poll found that 83% of the 1,005 US citizens polled blamed Hezbollah, at least in part, for the 2006 Lebanon War, compared to 66% who blamed Israel to some degree. Additionally, 76% disapproved of the military action Hezbollah took in Israel, compared to 38% who disapproved of Israel's military action in Lebanon.[^cite_note-Pollingreport-476] A poll in August 2006 by [*ABC News*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\)) and *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* found that 68% of the 1,002 US citizens blamed Hezbollah, at least in part, for the civilian casualties in Lebanon during the 2006 Lebanon War, compared to 31% who blamed Israel to some degree.[^cite_note-Pollingreport-476] Another August 2006 poll by *[CNN](62028.md)* showed that 69% of the 1,047 US citizens believed that Hezbollah is unfriendly towards, or an enemy of, the United States.[^cite_note-Pollingreport-476]

In 2010, a survey of Muslims in Lebanon showed that 94% of Lebanese [Shia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shi'a_Islam_in_Lebanon) supported Hezbollah, while 84% of the [Sunni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunni_Islam_in_Lebanon) Muslims held an unfavourable opinion of the group.[^cite_note-477]

Around 2012, some public opinion has started to turn against Hezbollah for their support of Syrian President Assad's attacks on the opposition movement in Syria.[^cite_note-478] Crowds in Cairo shouted out against Iran and Hezbollah, at a public speech by [Hamas](13913.md) President [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) in February 2012, when Hamas changed its support to the Syrian opposition.[^cite_note-479]

#### View of Hezbollah

A November 2020 poll in Lebanon performed by the pro-Israel, US [Washington Institute for Near East Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Institute_for_Near_East_Policy) declared that support for Hezbollah is declining significantly. Below is a table of the results of their polls.[^cite_note-480]

### Designation as a terrorist organisation or resistance movement

See also: [List of designated terrorist groups](63578.md)



Map of countries that designate Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation as of 2023

Hezbollah's status as a legitimate political party, a terrorist group, a resistance movement, or some combination thereof is a contentious issue.[^cite_note-horowitz1-481]

As of April 2026, Hezbollah or its military wing are considered [terrorist organisations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorist_organization) by at least 28 countries, as well as by the [European Union](9317.md) and since 2017 by most member states of the [Arab League](52625.md), with the exception of Iraq and Lebanon, where Hezbollah is the most powerful political party.[^cite_note-482] In June 2024, the Arab League leadership has announced that it no longer views Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-MEM-2024-483]

The countries that have designated Hezbollah a terrorist organisation include: the [Gulf Cooperation Council](220950.md) (GCC),[^cite_note-GCC-26] and their members Saudi Arabia,[^cite_note-WSJ-Gulf-Coop-39] Bahrain,[^cite_note-bahrain-list-484] United Arab Emirates,[^cite_note-WSJ-Gulf-Coop-39] as well as Argentina,[^cite_note-arg-repet-485] Canada,[^cite_note-canada-list-486] Colombia,[^cite_note-Anadolu-Agency-2020-18] Dominican Republic[^cite_note-487] Ecuador,[^cite_note-el-21] Estonia,[^cite_note-jpost646587-22] Germany,[^cite_note-488] Honduras,[^cite_note-Anadolu-Agency-2020-18] Israel,[^cite_note-489] Paraguay,[^cite_note-490][^cite_note-PAR-491] Kosovo,[^cite_note-492] Lithuania,[^cite_note-493] Serbia,[^cite_note-jpost646587-22] [Slovenia](27338.md),[^cite_note-Ahren-36] Switzerland,[^cite_note-494] United Kingdom,[^cite_note-homeoffice-495] United States,[^cite_note-us-fto-496] and Guatemala.[^cite_note-jpost646700-27]

The EU differentiates between Hezbollah's political wing and military wing, banning only the latter, though Hezbollah itself does not recognise such a distinction.[^cite_note-jpost646587-22] Hezbollah maintains that it is a legitimate [resistance movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Resistance_movement) fighting for the liberation of Lebanese territory.

There is a "wide difference" between US and Arab perception of Hezbollah.[^cite_note-nybooks-83] Several [Western countries](21208200.md) officially classify Hezbollah or its external security wing as a terrorist organisation, and some of their violent acts have been described as terrorist attacks. However, throughout most of the [Arab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_world) and [Muslim worlds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim_world), Hezbollah is referred to as a resistance movement, engaged in [national defence](240468.md).[^cite_note-HG20Ak02-144][^cite_note-hiof-Views-497][^cite_note-498] Even within Lebanon, sometimes Hezbollah's status as either a "militia" or "national resistance" has been contentious. In Lebanon, although not universally supported, Hezbollah is widely seen as a legitimate national resistance organisation defending Lebanon, and has been described by the Lebanese information minister as an important ally in fighting terrorist groups.[^cite_note-nybooks-83][^cite_note-499]

The [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) has never listed Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation under its sanctions list, although some of its members have done so individually. The [United Kingdom](31717.md) listed Hezbollah's military wing as a terrorist organisation[^cite_note-UKHO2015-500] until May 2019 when the entire organisation was proscribed,[^cite_note-UKban-40] and the [United States](3434750.md)[^cite_note-Designation-of-Foreign-Terrorist-Organizations-501] lists the entire group as such. [Russia](25391.md) has considered Hezbollah a legitimate sociopolitical organisation,[^cite_note-Russia-says-Hezbollah-502] and the [People's Republic of China](5405.md) remains neutral and maintains contacts with Hezbollah.[^cite_note-Nashabe-2012-503]

In May 2013, [France](5843419.md) and [Germany](11867.md) released statements that they will join other European countries in calling for an [EU](9317.md)-blacklisting of Hezbollah as a terror group.[^cite_note-504] In April 2020 Germany designated the organisation—including its political wing—as a terrorist organisation, and banned any activity in support of Hezbollah.[^cite_note-505]

In November 2025, [Iraq](7515928.md), by Resolution No. 61 of the 'Committee for the Freezing of Terrorists' Assets' of the [Central Bank of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Iraq), designated Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation and froze its assets. The decision was issued in the 4848th edition of the *[Official Gazette of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_Gazette_of_Iraq)*, on 18 November 2025.[^cite_note-Iraq_designates_Hezbollah-506][^cite_note-507] However, Iraqi officials subsequently called the decision a mistake, and the prime minister ordered an investigation.[^cite_note-MEE-508] On 15 July 2026, Iraq sanctioned Hezbollah and entities linked to it, a move which according to Iraqi officials was in accordance with the amended U.S. Executive Order 13224 targeting terrorism financing.[^cite_note-509]

On 8 April 2026, [Costa Rica](5551.md) designated Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-CostaRica-19]

On 15 April 2026, [Trinidad and Tobago](3565457.md) designated the IRGC as a terrorist organization.[^cite_note-510]

#### In the Western world

The [United States Department of State](31975.md) has designated Hezbollah a terrorist organisation since 1995. The group remains on Foreign Terrorist Organisation and Specially Designated Terrorist lists. According to the [Congressional Research Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congressional_Research_Service), "The US government holds Hezbollah responsible for...attacks and hostage takings targeting US citizens in Lebanon during the 1980s, including the [bombing of the US Embassy in Beirut in April 1983](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_US_embassy_bombing_in_Beirut) and the [bombing of the US Marine barracks in October 1983](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_Beirut_barracks_bombings), which together killed 258 US citizens. Hezbollah's operations outside of Lebanon, including its participation in bombings of Israeli and Jewish targets in Argentina during the 1990s and...training and liaison activities with Shiite insurgents in Iraq, have cemented the organisation's reputation among U.S. policy makers as a capable and deadly adversary with potential global reach."[^cite_note-529] In 2015, the US [Director of National Intelligence](456082.md) removed Hezbollah from the list of "active terrorist threats" while Hezbollah remained designated as terrorist by the US,[^cite_note-530] and Hezbollah officials were sanctioned for their role in facilitating military activity in the Syrian Civil War.[^cite_note-531] In May 2025, senior Hezbollah officials and financial facilitators around new sanctions, were targeted by the US, for their role in coordinating financial transfers to the Iran-backed group.[^cite_note-532] In April 2026, US ambassador to Lebanon [Michel Issa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michel_Issa) stated that Lebanon's formal designation of Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation was a prerequisite for US diplomatic intervention to end the war with Israel.[^cite_note-533]

The UK was the first government to attempt to make a distinction between Hezbollah's political and military wings, declaring the latter a terrorist group in 2008.[^cite_note-534] In 2019, the UK Government proscribed the entirety of Hezbollah as an organisation due to the difficulties in distinguishing between the political and military wings, as a way of limiting its influence in the UK.[^cite_note-535][^cite_note-536] In 2012, British Foreign Minister [William Hague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Hague) "urged the EU to place Hezbollah's military wing on its list of terrorist organisations".[^cite_note-537] The US urged the EU to classify Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation. In light of findings implicating Hezbollah in the [2012 Burgas bus bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Burgas_bus_bombing), Bulgaria, there was discussion within the EU to label Hezbollah's military wing as a terrorist group.[^cite_note-538] In 2013, the EU agreed to blacklist Hezbollah's military wing over its role in the Syrian conflict.[^cite_note-539][^cite_note-nytimes.com-540] The EU, France,[^cite_note-Algemeiner-Journal-2013-24] and [New Zealand](4913064.md) have proscribed Hezbollah's military wing, but do not list Hezbollah as a whole as a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-NZ-r1373-terrorlist-33][^cite_note-Rudoren-Jodi-2013a-23] During the Israeli occupation of southern Lebanon, French Prime Minister [Lionel Jospin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lionel_Jospin) stated that "France condemns Hezbollah's attacks, and all types of terrorist attacks which may be carried out against soldiers, or possibly Israel's civilian population."[^cite_note-541] Italian Foreign Minister [Massimo D'Alema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massimo_D'Alema) differentiated the wings of Hezbollah: "Apart from their well-known terrorist activities, they also have political standing and are socially engaged."[^cite_note-542] Germany does not maintain its list, having chosen to adopt the EU list. However, German officials have indicated they would support designating Hezbollah a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-543] The Netherlands regards Hezbollah as terrorist labelling it as such in reports of its intelligence and security service[^cite_note-nlfas-544] and in official answers by its Foreign Minister.[^cite_note-545] Serbia, which designated Iran-backed Hezbollah entirely as a terrorist organisation, fully implement measures to restrict Hezbollah's operations and financial activities.[^cite_note-english.alarabiya.net-35]

In the midst of the 2006 conflict between Hezbollah and Israel, Russia's government declined to include Hezbollah in a list of terrorist organisations stating that it lists only organisations which represent "the greatest threat to the security of our country".[^cite_note-546] Prior to the release of the list, Russian Defence Minister [Sergei Ivanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Ivanov) called "on Hezbollah to stop resorting to any terrorist methods, including attacking neighboring states".[^cite_note-547] Argentine prosecutors hold Hezbollah, and its financial supporters in Iran, responsible for the [1994 AMIA Bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_AMIA_Bombing) of a Jewish cultural centre, described as "the worst terrorist attack on Argentine soil", in which "85 people were killed and more than 200 others injured".[^cite_note-Argentine-454][^cite_note-548] The [GCC](220950.md),[^cite_note-GCC-26] [Canada](5042916.md),[^cite_note-h-17] [Israel](9282173.md),[^cite_note-Hezbollah_–_International_terrorist_organisation-29] and [Australia](4689264.md)[^cite_note-guardaust-14] have classified Hezbollah as a [terrorist organisation](63578.md).

The UN does not maintain a terrorist list,[^cite_note-549] however, it has made repeated calls for Hezbollah to disarm and accused the group of destabilising the region and causing harm to Lebanese civilians.[^cite_note-550][^cite_note-551][^cite_note-552] Human rights organisations [Amnesty International](18947898.md) and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) have accused Hezbollah of committing [war crimes](48626.md) against Israeli civilians.[^cite_note-553][^cite_note-554][^cite_note-555][^cite_note-556]

#### In the Arab and Muslim world



Protesters in [Kuala Lumpur](16854.md), Malaysia, with a Hezbollah flag in September 2012

In 2006, Hezbollah was regarded as a legitimate resistance movement throughout most of the Arab and Muslim world.[^cite_note-HG20Ak02-144] Furthermore, most of the Sunni Arab world sees Hezbollah as an agent of Iranian influence, and therefore, would like to see their power in Lebanon diminished.[^cite_note-557] [Egypt](8087628.md), Jordan, and [Saudi Arabia](349303.md) have condemned Hezbollah's actions, saying that "the Arabs and Muslims can't afford to allow an irresponsible and adventurous organisation like Hezbollah to drag the region to war" and calling it "dangerous adventurism".[^cite_note-The_Jerusalem_Post-558]

After an alleged [2009 Hezbollah plot in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_Hezbollah_plot_in_Egypt), the Egyptian regime of [Hosni Mubarak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hosni_Mubarak) officially classified Hezbollah as a terrorist group.[^cite_note-la-559] Following the 2012 Presidential elections the new government recognised Hezbollah as a "real political and military force" in Lebanon. The Egyptian ambassador to Lebanon, Ashraf Hamdy, stated that, "Resistance in the sense of defending Lebanese territory ... That's their primary role. We ... think that as a resistance movement they have done a good job to keep on defending Lebanese territory and trying to regain land occupied by Israel is legal and legitimate."[^cite_note-RK2012-560][^cite_note-LW2012-561]

During the [Bahraini uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahraini_uprising_\(2011%E2%80%93present\)), Bahrain [foreign minister](18478684.md) [Khalid ibn Ahmad Al Khalifah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalid_ibn_Ahmad_Al_Khalifah) labelled Hezbollah a terrorist group and accused them of supporting the protesters.[^cite_note-bahrain-562][^cite_note-563] On 10 April 2013, Bahrain blacklisted Hezbollah as a terrorist group, being the first Arab state in this regard.[^cite_note-jpost10apr13-564]

While Hezbollah has supported popular uprisings in [Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Egyptian_revolution), [Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Yemeni_uprising), Bahrain and [Tunisia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunisian_revolution), Hezbollah publicly sided with Iran and Syria during the [2011 Syrian uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_Syrian_uprising).[^cite_note-565] This position has prompted criticism from anti-government Syrians. As Hezbollah supported other movements in the context of the [Arab Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring), anti-government Syrians have stated that they feel "betrayed" by a double standard allegedly applied by the movement. Following Hezbollah's aid in Assad government's [victory in Qusayr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_al-Qusayr_\(2013\)), anti-Hezbollah editorials began regularly appearing in the Arabic media and anti-Hezbollah graffiti has been seen in southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-566]

In March 2016, the [GCC](220950.md) designated Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation due to its alleged attempts to undermine GCC states, and [Arab League](52625.md) followed the move, with reservation by [Iraq](7515928.md) and [Lebanon](17771.md). In the summit, Lebanese Foreign Minister [Gebran Bassil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gebran_Bassil) said that "Hezbollah enjoys wide representation and is an integral faction of the Lebanese community", while Iraqi Foreign Minister [Ibrahim al-Jaafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibrahim_al-Jaafari) said [PMF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hashd_al-Shaabi) and Hezbollah "have preserved Arab dignity" and those who accuse them of being terrorists are terrorists themselves. The Saudi delegation walked out of the meeting.[^cite_note-567] Israel's Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) called the step, "'important' and 'even amazing'".[^cite_note-568]

A day before the move by the Arab League, Hezbollah leader Nasrallah said that, "Saudi Arabia is angry with Hezbollah since it is daring to say what only a few others dare to say against [its royal family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Saud)."[^cite_note-569]

In 2020, a German security contractor accused [Qatar](8238258.md) of financing Hezbollah.[^cite_note-570] In September 2021, U.S' Secretary of State, [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken) commended the combined efforts taken by the [United States](3434750.md) and the [Government of Qatar](25190.md) against Hezbollah financial network which involved the abuse of international financial system by using global networks of financiers and front companies to spread [terrorism](30636.md).[^cite_note-571] In July 2022, Qatar participated in a 30-nation meeting led by the United States to counter Hezbollah, according to [Axios](52436164.md).[^cite_note-572]

In June 2024, the Arab League Assistant Secretary-General Hossam Zaki has announced that the Arab League no longer considers Hezbollah a terrorist organisation.[^cite_note-MEM-2024-483]

#### In Lebanon



Graves of killed Hezbollah members at a mausoleum in Beirut

In an interview during the 2006 Lebanon War, then-President [Emile Lahoud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emile_Lahoud) stated, "Hezbollah enjoys utmost prestige in Lebanon, because it freed our country ... even though it is very small, it stands up to Israel."[^cite_note-573] Following the 2006 War, other Lebanese including members of the government were resentful of the large damage sustained by the country and saw Hezbollah's actions as unjustified "dangerous adventurism" rather than legitimate resistance. They accused Hezbollah of acting on behalf of Iran and Syria.[^cite_note-574] An official of the [Future Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Future_Movement), part of the [March 14 Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_14_Alliance), warned that Hezbollah "has all the characteristics of a terrorist party", and that Hezbollah is moving Lebanon toward the Iranian Islamic system of government.[^cite_note-575]

In August 2008, Lebanon's cabinet completed a policy statement which recognised "the right of Lebanon's people, army, and resistance to liberate the Israeli-occupied Shebaa Farms, Kafar Shuba Hills, and the Lebanese section of Ghajar village, and defend the country using all legal and possible means".[^cite_note-Nafez_Qawas-576]

[Gebran Tueni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gebran_Tueni), a late conservative Orthodox Christian editor of [an-Nahar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/An-Nahar), referred to Hezbollah as an "Iranian import" and said "they have nothing to do with Arab civilisation". Tuení believed that Hezbollah's evolution is cosmetic, concealing a sinister long-term strategy to Islamicise Lebanon and lead it into a ruinous war with Israel.[^cite_note-nybooks-83]

By 2017, a poll showed that 62% of Lebanese Christians believed that Hezbollah was doing a "better job than anyone else in defending Lebanese interests in the region, and they trust it more than other social institutions".[^cite_note-577]

#### Scholarly views

Academics specialising in a wide variety of the social sciences believe that Hezbollah is an example of an Islamic terrorist organisation. Such scholars and research institutes include the following:

- [Walid Phares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walid_Phares), Lebanese-born terrorism scholar and member of the Lebanese [Kataeb Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kataeb_Party)

  [^cite_note-578]

  [^cite_note-579]

  [^cite_note-580]

  [^cite_note-581]

  [^cite_note-582]

  [^cite_note-583]

  [^cite_note-Gunaratna-463]

- Neeru Gaba, Australian scholar

  [^cite_note-584]

- Tore Bjørgo, Norwegian scholar

  [^cite_note-585]

- Magnus Norell, of the [European Foundation for Democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Foundation_for_Democracy)

  [^cite_note-586]

- [Anthony Cordesman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Cordesman), of the US [Center for Strategic and International Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Strategic_and_International_Studies)

### Views of foreign legislators

[J. Gresham Barrett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Gresham_Barrett) brought up legislation in the US House of Representatives which, among other things, referred to Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation. Congress members [Tom Lantos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Lantos), [Jim Saxton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Saxton), [Thad McCotter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thad_McCotter), [Chris Shays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Shays), [Charles Boustany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Boustany), [Alcee Hastings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcee_Hastings), and [Robert Wexler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Wexler) referred to Hezbollah as a terrorist organisation in their speeches supporting the legislation.[^cite_note-590] Shortly before a speech by Iraqi Prime Minister [Nouri al-Maliki](4723094.md), U.S. Congressman [Dennis Hastert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dennis_Hastert) said, "He \[Maliki] denounces terrorism, and I have to take him at his word. Hezbollah is a terrorist organisation."[^cite_note-591]

In 2011, a bipartisan group of members of US Congress introduced the Hezbollah Anti-Terrorism Act. The act ensures that no US aid to Lebanon will enter the hands of Hezbollah. On the day of the act's introduction, Congressman [Darrell Issa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darrell_Issa) said, "Hezbollah is a terrorist group and a cancer on Lebanon. The Hezbollah Anti-Terrorism Act surgically targets this cancer and will strengthen the position of Lebanese who oppose Hezbollah."[^cite_note-592]

In a Sky News interview during the 2006 Lebanon war, British MP [George Galloway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Galloway) said that Hezbollah is "not a terrorist organisation".[^cite_note-593]

Former Swiss member of parliament, [Jean Ziegler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Ziegler), said in 2006: "I refuse to describe Hezbollah as a terrorist group. It is a national movement of resistance."[^cite_note-594]

Hezbollah was founded in 1982, as violence against U.S. troops in Lebanon spiked. In addition to its roles in the major bombings of 1983, the militant group was involved in the bombing of the U.S. Embassy annex in Beirut in 1984 that killed 23 people, the hijacking of TWA Flight 847 in 1985 and the Khobar Towers attack in Saudi Arabia in 1996 that killed 19 U.S. airmen, according to the Office of the Director of National Intelligence.
